# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- tests: a golden characterization of the node classes (P17-0): what each node type's script handle (its proxy) and its class do with a fixed list of values, written as text, one file per type in `test/golden/nodes/` (Node and the 23 exports of `src/core/nodes/index.js`). A later change to `src/core/nodes/` shows as a diff of those files, to be read line by line. The files record the behaviour as it is, defects included: a line there does not say the behaviour is right, and files that stay the same show only that none of the recorded cases changed, not that a change is safe.
  - **What is recorded.** (a) The proxy's own keys in order; for each its kind, enumerability, configurability, its functions' names and lengths, and whether two instances share a function (each setter, through the run binding) or each has its own (getters, methods). (b) A stopped run: what every setter, method and getter of the proxy does once the node's run is over, on a new node each time, with the node's own fields it changed and the calls made on it; the setters and methods also through `_created` and through a parent's context; a live run; a setter called on `Object.create(proxy)`, on a plain object, on no receiver, on a group's proxy and on another type's proxy. (c) One set on a fresh unmounted node for every settable key, through the proxy and through the class, of a hostile list (undefined, null, NaN, ±Infinity, -1, -0, 0, 0.5, 1, 1e9, `''`, `'x'`, true, false, `{}`, arrays, boxed primitives, a Proxy over an array that lists what is read of it, a function, a Symbol, a BigInt, an object whose `toString` throws) and the property's own values (its enum, a vector, an array of its shape, a fixture): the error, or the value read back (and whether it is a new object on every read, frozen or sealed), the node's own fields that changed, the calls of `setDirty`, `rebuild`, `redraw`, `activate`, … on it, the console. (d) A second set (the same value, an equal new object, NaN twice, 0 then -0), which shows whether and how a setter compares before its effect. (e) The same sets with the parts a setter looks for on a mounted node stubbed (a handle, a yoga node and its UI at two resolutions, an audio source with its gain and panner, a video instance, a collider shape, an emitter, a controller, an avatar instance, a parent and a context; the mesh and collider sizes for each shape type, the prim size for each prim type), with what was written to or called on each stub; only what differs from (c) is written. (f) The constructor: the defaults, and each key given through `data` beside the same value set through the class. (g) The node's own fields after construction (with their descriptor where it is not a plain field's); what `clone()` and `clone(true)` make of each own field, for a node with every property set, its own transform, id and owning run, for the nodes `glbToNodes` makes from a small scene (each with a transform of its own) and for the host-only fields other loaders give; the fields a dropped `copy()` line would leave unchanged; and that a clone reads as its source for each property set alone, which the test also asserts. (h) Two nodes of a type alive at once, their proxies kept: a write through each one's in turn (a second value that reads otherwise than the first: a callback of another name, or the default), read back through both kept proxies, again after each node's `getProxy()` was asked once more, and the other node's own fields compared before and after, so that a setter or a getter that reaches another node shows; the keys that take a value only on a mounted node (audio `currentTime`, video `time` and `currentTime`) paired with a stateful stand-in on each node; the keys no script sets (get-only, or a setter that takes no value) read through the kept proxies of two nodes in distinct states. (i) Every getter on a node whose backing values are distinct: under a positioned parent (its `matrix` is not its `matrixWorld`), with two children, its own transform and callbacks, and the type's computed state (a playing audio source, a video instance with its own playback fields, a joint's frames apart from the node's transform, a controller's collision flags, …). Two getters that read the same object, or two getters of keys no script sets that read the same value, fail the test unless they are a listed alias; each alias is read again with its target key redefined on the proxy and must still reach the node. (x) What the console said outside the cases, by row (the generator's own probing for sample values, a case's set-up, and, as `setup`, the bundle's evaluation and the generator's fixtures: the capture starts before the bundle is imported), so that a once-only warning said there first is written too.
  - **The files.** `test/lib/nodeGolden.mjs` generates. `test/nodeGolden.test.js` (with the legend of the rows and what it cannot see at its top) and `test/nodeGolden{Physics,Scene,Effects,Ui,View}.test.js` each generate a share of the types and compare them, and a failure prints the first differing lines; `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` rewrites the files, and is refused when `CI` is in the environment at all, empty included (a failing test; nothing is generated or written). Each node type is generated from its own evaluation of a bundle of `src/` (its once-only warnings start unsaid; the packages three.js, three-mesh-bvh, yoga-layout and lodash are shared), in a fixed order; nothing written carries an address, a time, a path or an automatic id. A string of more than 40 characters, an array of more than 16 items, what lies more than three levels deep and more than 24 reads of a Proxy are shortened, with the first 12 hex digits of the sha256 of the whole text in their place. Node 22 and Node 24 write the same text.
  - **Size and time.** 6,444 lines (563,760 bytes), from 64 (anchor) to 650 (uiview). Run side by side on their own, the six files generate in 1.6 to 1.9 s each (Node 22, five runs). Inside `npm test`, over three runs that began at a load average of 7 on 10 cores (the suite took 36.6 to 37.9 s), each generated in 2.8 to 4.7 s. One file for all the types took 11 s in `npm test`; split in three, the slowest took 10.7 s under a load average of 28 on 10 cores, hence six.
  - **Mutants.** 66 hand mutants of the node classes in 19 files, each making the test fail: a message, a default, an accepted value, a normalizer, each of the five ways a setter compares (`===`, none, lodash `isEqual`, `Vector3.equals`, `===` against a fresh frozen copy), an effect removed or moved before the comparison, a dropped `copy()` line (the position, scale, quaternion and owning run in `Node.copy()`, a joint's frame among them), a proxy key order swap, a key added after `this.proxy =`, a getter made shared, a setter that uses `this` instead of `self`, the constructor's order, a setter the run binding skips, the validation moved after the assignment, a script's array read twice, a setter that writes another node of its type, a getter that reads a neighbouring field (`matrixWorld` the `matrix`, a video's `playing` its `loop`, …), a setter or method that acts before the stopped-run check, a change inside a shortened text, a once-only warning said first by the generator's own probing, by its fixtures or while the bundle is evaluated, an own field made non-enumerable, a getter that reads the node of the latest `getProxy()`, a setter of a key that takes a value only when mounted writing another node, a setter that also writes the previous instance, a getter that reads the node's own transform, an alias that reads its target through the proxy.
  - **Found, not fixed** (in the files as they are, unless said otherwise). Reading an audio node's `currentTime` before it is placed in a world throws a TypeError (`this.ctx` is null, `Audio.js` 307); so do its `pause()` and `stop()` (401, 414; seen by a probe, the files record methods only in a stopped run). `prim.emissiveIntensity` took NaN and +Infinity, and particles `direction` and `velocityRadial` NaN and ±Infinity: fixed meanwhile by a change of its own (the entry under Fixed, "(P17)"), and the regenerated `prim.txt` and `particles.txt` are its record: 22 lines in and 17 out, every one of them a line of those three properties (rows c, d, e and f): their `NaN` and infinity outcomes, and the regrouping of the other values' lines and the counts ("the other n as in c") that follow from them. A rigid body's proxy has setters but no getters for `linearDamping` and `angularDamping`: a script reads `undefined` back. Particles `onEnd` keeps any value, and nothing reads it; `rateOverDistance` drops a value it refuses without an error, unlike every other setter. Node `active` keeps any primitive given through the proxy (a number, a string, a Symbol, a BigInt), while its message says boolean and the constructor takes a boolean only. A UI's `offset` setter rebuilds twice (the vector's change callback, then the setter). The joint constructor keeps any truthy `type` (`app.create` sets it again through the proxy, which refuses it). By reading only: `UI.unmount` clears `needRebuild`, not `needsRebuild` (`UI.js` 424), and the proxy's pointer-callback getters look at the proxy's own `ctx`, which it never has (`Node.js` 838; the class getter does the check). Fixed since, each by itself (the entry under Fixed, "(P17-F2)"): the audio node's three, the rigid body's getters, `UI.unmount` and the pointer-callback getters; with the regenerated `audio.txt`, `rigidbody.txt` and `prim.txt` as their record.
  - **What it cannot see.** What a mount, a commit or a build makes (three.js objects, the UI canvas and what is drawn on it, Yoga's layout, the WebAudio graph, PhysX, the particle worker); anything asynchronous (a load and what follows it); the `isBrowser` gates (it runs without `window`); SES lockdown and compartments; the live behaviour of the proxy's methods (only their descriptors, and that a stopped run refuses them); values outside its lists, combinations of properties beyond the stubbed and distinct states, a second set other than the four pairs; a once-only warning after the first place that says it (a case, or row x); a getter called with another receiver (only setters are); writes through an object a getter returns (only whether a getter returns a new object on every read); a proxy getter that reads the node's private field where the class getter only returns that field; a mistake that shows on a third node only; of a thrown error, more than its class and message; of an accessor, more than its name, length, descriptor and sharing; memory and speed.
- ci: a workflow of its own builds the Docker image and runs it: `.github/workflows/image.yml`, with `scripts/image-check.mjs`. Until now no job built the image by itself (`docker.yml` publishes it by hand). Neither the image nor production changes.
  - **What the job checks.** `docker build` of the repository. A container started as the image starts (its own entry point and command, a named volume at `/app/world`, made-up settings, no port published) becomes `healthy`, runs a `node build/index.js` process and answers `GET /status` with the build's `COMMIT_HASH`. `docker stop -t 10` (SIGTERM) and, on a second container, `docker stop --signal SIGINT -t 5` (Fly's documented default signal and timeout) each end with exit code 0, within 8 s and 4 s by Docker's own clock (its `kill` event to the container's `die`, from `docker events`; when those cannot be read, the `docker stop` command's own time within the whole 10 s and 5 s), with the server's `saving the world before exit` and `[network] final save complete` lines in the log and none of the lines that say that something was not stopped or not saved (`[network] not persisted`, `[server] stopping the world failed`, `… was not loaded; changes will not be saved` among them: `NOT_STOPPED_OR_SAVED`, each quoted from its source), whatever the exit code. The exit code is the container's, that is PID 1's (npm in today's image): the server's own is not seen. A third container on the same volume becomes healthy; nothing having been changed in the world, that does not show that what was saved is read back.
  - **What it records.** Who PID 1 is and which of the two signals it handles, whether a shell sits between it and the server, the uids and the image's configuration; for each stop the signals Docker sent, the `die` event's exit code and how long the `docker stop` command took: in the job's output and in the `image-check` artifact, with each container's log.
  - **When it runs.** For a pull request or a push to `main` that changes the Dockerfile, `.dockerignore`, `package.json`, the lock file, `.nvmrc`, `scripts/build*.mjs`, `scripts/lib/`, `src/server/`, `src/core/` (bundled into the server, the final save among it), the script or the workflow; weekly; by hand. It is not a required check (a path-filtered one stays pending on every other pull request). It has `contents: read`, uses no Docker action, runs no `npm ci` and pushes nothing. The step runs the script with `exec`, so that a cancelled run's SIGINT reaches Node rather than Bash.
  - **Not covered.** What Fly does differently from `docker run` (its own init, its signal and timeout, who owns a volume, its health checks), other architectures, a world with content: DOCKER.md, "Checked in CI". Nothing is changed in the world before the stops.
  - **The script.** `--model=current` is the image as it is today and the only model; an unknown one is an error. `--dry-run` prints the docker commands of a run without running any. What it creates carries a random id of the run and is removed at the end, also after a failure or a signal. A printed command shows the value of a setting whose name says it is secret (`SECRET`, `KEY`, `TOKEN`, `PASSWORD`, `CODE`) as `***`; docker is given the real one, made up for the run.
  - **A cancelled run.** GitHub sends the step SIGINT, SIGTERM 7.5 s later, and kills it 2.5 s after that. On the first signal the docker command in flight is ended unless it creates or removes something (a `docker stop` of a container that does not stop, a `logs`, an `exec`, the build, the wait between health polls), and the cleanup starts at once: a `docker run` or `volume create` in flight gets 1.5 s, each of the three removals 2 s, 7.5 s in all. A second signal does not stop the cleanup. The exit code is 130 or 143, after the first signal.
  - **Tests.** `test/imageCheck.test.js`: the BusyBox `ps` tree, `/proc/1/status`, the verdict on a stop and the order of the docker commands, on canned output and against a stand-in for the docker CLI, and a SIGINT while that stand-in's `docker stop` never returns (the stop is killed, the three removals run in order) and a second signal during the cleanup. Each line that fails a stop is produced by the real signal handler (`src/server/shutdown.js`) where it can be, else written as the source prints it; a source-text tripwire fails on a log call in `installShutdown` or on the final save's path in `ServerNetwork.js` that the list neither forbids nor allows with a reason. No test runs Docker.
  - `scripts/prod-smoke.mjs` takes the server's settings from the module the image check uses (`scripts/lib/startEnv.mjs`); the settings are the same as before.
- Tips (beta): a maximum per tip, 1,000 JPYC and 10 USDC for the built-in tokens. A custom token spec can give one, `SYMBOL:0xaddress:decimals[:min[:max]]`.
  - **Where it is checked.** The client checks the minimum and the maximum when Next is pressed, before the confirmation step. The server refuses a larger intent with `above_maximum`.
  - **The form.** It shows the range in the amount field and a beta notice: start small, from a wallet holding only a little.
  - **Design.** goals/phase8-tip-beta-limits-design.md.
- docs: the terms of service, dated 2026-11-10 (still before launch, with no users, so updated in place):
  - tips are called 投げ銭;
  - Article 3 says tips are a beta feature developed with the help of AI. The per-tip maximum is shown in the tip form (currently 1,000 JPYC). A transfer whose recipient and amount do not match a tip the Service accepted is not a tip;
  - Article 8 limits liability concerning tips, except for intent or gross negligence, to the greater of the amount the tips causing the damage were meant to send and the amount actually sent (their total, if several).
  The English translation follows. The EVM wallet panel also tells users that tips are in beta and to connect a wallet holding only a small amount.
- client, tips: the tip form tells the payer more about who is paid and what is known (`src/core/extras/tipOutcome.js`). All of it is information for the payer, not a control: anything running in the page can change both the form and the request the wallet gets. The form limits no amount either: the built-in tokens have a maximum per tip (1,000 JPYC, 10 USDC), a custom token has one only if its spec gives it (`:max`), and none of this limits what code running in the page asks a wallet for.
  - **The recipient's short address.** The sending step shows the recipient's name and short address (`0x1563…5508`) once the server's tip intent has arrived, and the "Sent" and "may have been sent" texts carry it. For an ERC-20 transfer a wallet shows the token contract as "to", and some mobile wallets do not decode the recipient at all: the form says that the wallet may show the token contract and may not show this address. The address is disclosed to the sender at the same moment as before (the intent). In these texts the name is shown without the bidirectional control characters U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069, U+200E, U+200F and U+061C, and the form renders it in a `<bdi>` of its own and the address in an element of its own with `dir="ltr"` (a name with U+202E in it reordered the address and the sentence after it). The name elsewhere in the interface (the form's title and confirmation sentence, the Players list, nametags, chat) is shown as before.
  - **Same name.** When another connected player has the recipient's name, the form says so. Two names are the same after Unicode NFKC (full-width `Ｂｏｂ` folds to `Bob`), collapsing every run of white space to one space (tabs, line breaks and the Unicode spaces included, so `Bob<tab>Smith` is `Bob Smith`: this comes first, as a tab is a control character too), removing characters that are not drawn (zero-width and other default-ignorable, control and format characters), lower-casing, and trimming. **Not caught:** look-alike letters of another script (a Cyrillic `В` and `о` in "Bob"), and any name that only resembles another. The payer's own name does not count.
  - **A note of the request.** Just before the wallet is asked to send, the browser writes a note to `localStorage` under a key of its own, `evmTipRequest:` and an id made for the note. A note holds the recipient's display name, the short address, the amount, the token symbol, the time of that moment and that random id: not the full address, not the payer's address, not a transaction hash. That key is removed when the transaction hash has been handed to the server or the wallet explicitly rejected, and no other: nothing is read to decide what to remove, so a note another tab writes meanwhile stays; a request that never reached the wallet writes none. The ten minutes count from the request to the wallet, not from the intent (the account and network prompts before it can take minutes). A note that is left (a page reloaded on the way back from a wallet app, which iOS often does; a deploy or a lost connection seconds after a send; an answer that was not clear) is shown under the wallet button, whether the panel is open or folded and before any sign-in: "A tip may have been sent: 100 JPYC to Bob (0x1563…5508). … Check your wallet's activity before sending it again.", each until its own Dismiss (which removes that note's key, by the id shown) or until it is ten minutes old. The first four notes are shown, newest first, and a line says how many more there are ("2 more notes like these are kept in this browser. They are shown here as these are dismissed."); dismissing one brings the next into view. A note older than ten minutes is removed when a page of this site next reads the notes: nothing removes it while no such page is open, so it can stay in the browser's storage until then, or until the browser clears the site's data. A read never removes a well-formed note younger than ten minutes; under the prefix it removes the expired ones and what is not a note: malformed, longer than 2,048 characters, kept under a key that is not its own, or timed more than ten minutes ahead of the reader's clock (one up to ten minutes ahead is kept and expires ten minutes after its own time). A read finds every note that the storage lets it read, whatever else is under the prefix, and the page reads again a moment after another tab changes this site's storage (a retry, which can be cut short like the first read); the count the notice gives is of the notes that were read, and it says "at least" when a value could not be. A page learns of another tab's note when it next reads the storage (its load, the end of a send of its own, a Dismiss, a note's ten minutes). Anything running on this origin in this browser profile can read the notes, including other pages and worlds served from the same origin; other sites cannot. It is this browser's memory of a request, not a record of a payment: it says nothing about what the wallet did, another browser never sees it, and storage that is blocked or cleared means no note, as a full one can for a new note (the form and the send do not depend on it).
  - **A world that does not confirm.** The world tells its clients whether it confirms tips: `confirmsTips` in the `evmWalletStatus` of a successful sign-in (only where tips are on) and `confirms` in each accepted `evmTipIntent`. It is `false` when the server has no `EVM_RPC_URL_<chainId>` for the sign-in chain, and from the moment its RPC left the `finalized` tag unanswered (tips end `no_finality`) until a later read of the tag works. The form then says, on the confirmation step and again on the sending step, before the wallet is asked: "This world is not confirming tips right now. The transfer still goes through your wallet and may not be confirmed or announced here." ("May not": after a `no_finality` a later read of the tag can work, and a tip sent meanwhile can still be confirmed and announced.) When the intent is the first to say so (the world had said yes at sign-in, or nothing), the send stops on a step that says it, before the wallet is asked anything (no account, network or transfer request), and goes on only when the payer presses **Send anyway**; Cancel goes back to the amount. Send anyway acts on that intent for 5 minutes of time elapsed in the page since the intent arrived (`performance.now()`, which a change of the wall clock does not move; the server keeps the tip request for 10 minutes from issuing it, and the intent carries neither time): later, the wallet is asked nothing and the form says "This request expired before you answered. Nothing was sent. Start again.", back at the amount, where Next opens the confirmation and Send tip asks for a new intent. A send without that step goes on at once and is not timed. A sender used to learn this only after paying. A tip of the player's own that comes back `no_rpc` or `no_finality` counts as the world's word too, until the next sign-in or intent says otherwise. A world that has not said (an older server) shows nothing.
  - **Tests.** `test/tipOutcome.test.js` (the outcome, the note and its id, the names with the Cyrillic miss asserted and tabs, line breaks and no-break spaces, the texts and their parts with bidirectional controls in a name), `test/tipForm.test.js` (the form's steps, the "not confirming" step, how a name and an address are rendered, and the notice), `test/evmClient.test.js` (two tabs that share the note, a network prompt of eight minutes, the order of the wallet requests around the "not confirming" step), `test/serverEvmWallet.test.js`, `test/evmTipWatcher.test.js`; `test/e2e/evm-tips-gate.mjs` with a mock wallet that holds, fails after taking, or never answers the request, and a mock RPC that stops answering the `finalized` tag after the sender signed in (the form stops before the wallet is asked anything, then sends on Send anyway). Not tried: any real wallet, phone or relay.
- server: the world page is now tried against a full Content-Security-Policy, as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`. **This protects nothing**: a browser loads and runs everything exactly as before, and posts what the policy would have refused. It is here to learn what enforcing the policy would break for real visitors (a wallet extension that puts a script into the page, a wallet's in-app browser, a frame a wallet library opens) before anything is enforced; the four directives that were already enforced are unchanged.
  - **The policy.** `default-src 'self'`; scripts from the page's own origin, with the response's nonce (the page's two inline scripts carry it; a new one each response, and the page now says `Cache-Control: no-cache`), or from the script editor's folder on cdnjs, with `'unsafe-eval'` (app scripts are evaluated); styles from the page's origin, inline, or that folder; images, media and connections from anywhere; workers from the page's origin and `blob:`; frames from WalletConnect's two Verify hosts only. Enforced, it would stop an inline script or handler that got into the page and scripts of other hosts. It would not stop text that reaches an `eval` of the page's own code, injected CSS, data leaving through an image or a request, or whoever controls that cdnjs folder; and app scripts are not limited by it at all.
  - **The reports.** `POST /csp-report`: 20 posts a minute from one address (an IPv6 address counting with its /64) and 600 a minute in all, counted when the request reaches the route and whatever the post turns out to be, the rest answered 429; the route keeps and parses a body of 8 KiB at most as sent, and a compressed one is refused (415), never decompressed. A post refused for the rate, its encoding, its type or its length is answered with `Connection: close`. These bound what the route does with a post, not what the HTTP layer reads from a connection: bytes that are sent are read and thrown away in cases no route decides, here as on every path of the server. The server writes a line the first time an hour sees a kind of report, again at 10, 100, 1000 of it, and one line of totals for an hour that had a report or a post over the rate: `[csp] report-only: <directive> would refuse <origin, scheme or the browser's word>, asked by <path of a file of the page, or an origin or scheme>`. An hour holds 100 kinds; more are only counted. No query string, page address, referrer, script sample or extension id is kept. Anyone can post to this route: a line is what was posted, not proof of what a browser did, and someone can fill an hour's table with made-up kinds.
  - **Not covered.** The other static pages (`terms.html`) are not tried against this policy. Only the old `report-uri` form is sent (every engine posts it; the newer Reporting API is not used). The end-to-end runs (the world, uploads, the script editor with its workers, an injected wallet, the WalletConnect sheet, tips) report nothing in Chromium and WebKit; real wallet extensions, wallet in-app browsers, WalletConnect's relay and Verify frame against the real service, and voice were not tried and are what the reports are for.
- docs: `docs/scripting/examples/nft-door.js`, a door that opens for holders of an OpenPay license NFT (ERC-1155 `0x1c652241Cc820Ce897F0d8D9351Df9AD8a0916cc` on Polygon). It is preset for the "Coo-World NFT Gate" product. It is a separate door built from the "TOKEN GATE COO" door's model and animation; TOKEN GATE COO and its Solana COO check stay as they are.
  - **The check.** It runs in the server script with `player.getEvmHolding()` and answers only the asking player. Each answer carries the request's build id, number and Open flag, so only the answer to that exact Open press opens the door. Answers to an earlier build, or after a wallet change, are status only.
  - **Without a pass.** The pass panel links to the product's OpenPay page in a new tab, and the sign shows the store's address.
  - **Design.** goals/phase7-openpay-nft-door-design.md.
- docs: terms of service at `/terms.html` (operator dWebXR Lab, contact @coo_aiagent on X), modeled on OpenPay's terms. The Japanese text governs; an English translation follows on the same page, and the in-world Terms links open it (`#en`).
  - **What they say.** The world is non-custodial and takes no part in transfers between players. Minors need a guardian's consent.
  - **Tips.**
    - **The address.** The opt-in is visible to the players in the world. The recipient's address goes to a sender when they start a tip, even one they never send, and it cannot be taken back.
    - **Sending.** Transfers go wallet to wallet on Polygon with no fee; the sender pays gas. There are per-token minimums and rate limits, and transfers cannot be undone.
    - **Confirmation.** "Could not confirm" does not mean the tip was not sent.
    - **Announcements.** Confirmed tips are announced in chat with display names, amount and token, sometimes to the recipient only. World apps are told the same with player ids. Neither gets addresses or hashes.
    - **No goods.** Tips buy nothing; an app's own perks are its author's responsibility. Recipients handle their own taxes.
  - **Data.** Wallet address, verified state and the tips setting are kept only in server memory, for the checks (sign-in, tip confirmation, token and NFT holdings). They are never stored in the database and are gone when the server restarts. RPC and WalletConnect get what they need to check.
  - **The rest.** Third-party services, suspension for violations, a liability limit for ordinary negligence, changes under Civil Code art. 548-4, Japanese law with the Tokyo District Court.
  - **Links.** The Receive tips switch and the tip confirmation link the terms. Both now say the world takes no fee, and the confirmation also says it is not a party to the transfer. Keys in the tips block stay out of the world's controls, so Tab reaches the link and Enter opens it without the chat; Escape still folds the panel.
- wallet: smart-contract wallets (EIP-1271, and EIP-6492 for accounts not deployed yet, such as Myna Wallet) can sign in with EVM when the server has `EVM_RPC_URL_<chainId>` for the sign-in chain.
  - **Check.** EOA signatures are still checked locally. Any other signature gets one deployless `eth_call` of the ERC-6492 universal validator at `latest`. The bytecode is copied from viem 2.57.8 and pinned in tests. The call is limited to 10M gas and 8 s, at most 4 at once and about 30 a minute per server.
  - **Results.** A revert means a wrong signature. A rate limit, timeout or unexpected answer means the check is unavailable, logged once per kind.
  - **Reasons.** `evmWalletStatus.reason` can now also be `contract_unsupported` (no RPC for the chain) or `unavailable`, each with its own message. The packet table and `PROTOCOL_VERSION` are unchanged.
  - **Signatures.** A sign-in signature may be 1 to 4096 bytes; the `requestEvmVerify` inbound cap rises from 512 to 8704 bytes.
- wallet: the WalletConnect phone links are MetaMask, HashPort Wallet and Myna Wallet (JPYC wallets), with the links from Reown's wallet registry. Trust Wallet and Rainbow are no longer listed.
- wallet: WalletConnect (Reown) sign-in for EVM wallets on phones and other devices, behind `PUBLIC_WALLETCONNECT_PROJECT_ID` (32 hex characters; empty keeps it off).
  - **Panel.** A WalletConnect button joins the announced wallets. Desktops get a QR code; phones get MetaMask / Trust Wallet / Rainbow links (plus the plain `wc:` link on Android), a QR toggle and Copy link. While a signature or tip waits, the panel says "Approve in your wallet app" with an Open wallet link on phones.
  - **Loading.** `@walletconnect/sign-client` 2.26.0 and `uqr` 0.1.3 load in their own chunk only when WalletConnect is picked (the client build now splits code; the particles worker stays one file).
  - **Behaviour.** An adapter on SignClient makes the session look like an injected wallet to the existing SIWE and tip code: accounts on the sign-in chain only (following the wallet's account switches), the network switch answered locally, rejections as 4001, and a session ended in the wallet seen as a disconnect. Each pairing has its own link; the relay gets 20 s to answer; Cancel (also while the sign-in waits in the wallet) ends the pending pairing and closes a session that arrives late. Sessions are ended by topic, waiting at most 10 s for the relay before they are dropped locally. A wallet's link back is used only when it is an https link or an app scheme.
  - Telemetry is off; the verification flow and server are unchanged.
- wallet: tips are confirmed on chain and announced (P2b) when the server has `EVM_RPC_URL_<chainId>` for the sign-in chain.
  - **Reporting.** Each tip intent now carries a server ticket. After the wallet returns the transaction hash, the client reports `{ ticket, txHash }` (new packets `evmTip` / `evmTipStatus`, appended; protocol version unchanged).
  - **Checking.** The server polls a shared, budgeted watcher. It matches only the token's `Transfer` log (sender, recipient, exact amount), not `receipt.from` or `receipt.to`, so EIP-7702 and sponsored transactions work. It waits for the `finalized` block, checks the block hash, and refuses transactions from a block more than about 30 s older than the ticket.
  - **Results.** Only the sender gets `watching`, then `confirmed`, `failed` or `unverified`. An unconfirmed tip can be reported again once.
  - **Duplicates.** A sender's transfer in a transaction is counted once while the server process remembers it (see Fixed), and is only marked seen when confirmed, so another player's report of the same hash cannot block it.
  - **Announcement.** A confirmed tip is announced as a system chat message, paced to 3 at once then one per 3 s, and one per sender per 30 s; otherwise only the recipient is told.
  - **Script event.** Server scripts get the protected `tip` event, with no addresses or transaction hash.
  - **RPC errors.** `RpcError` now separates JSON-RPC error answers from transport failures.
- wallet: tips between players (JPYC / USDC on Polygon) behind `PUBLIC_EVM_TIP_TOKENS`. A verified player opts in with **Receive tips**; other players see only an `evmTips` flag and a tip button in the Players list. Sending asks the server for a tip intent (verified senders only, 3 per socket then 1 per 10 s, never to the same wallet, per-token minimum) that returns the recipient's verified address and the exact amount; the sender's wallet then sends one ERC-20 `transfer` straight to that address after checking its account and chain (switching to, or adding, Polygon). The server never holds funds or builds transactions, and tips are not yet verified on-chain or announced.
- apps: `player.getEvmHolding(spec)` for server scripts checks whether a player's verified EVM wallet holds an ERC-721 / ERC-1155 token through a server-only `EVM_RPC_URL_<chainId>` (cached 30 s, shared budget, fail-closed `known: false`), and the protected server event `evmWallet` reports verification changes. Example app: `docs/scripting/examples/nft-gate.js` (works with OpenPay license NFT ids).
- wallet: EVM wallets (MetaMask, Rabby, any EIP-6963 or injected `window.ethereum` wallet) can prove address ownership next to Solana, behind `PUBLIC_EVM_CHAIN_IDS` (for example `137`; empty keeps EVM off and the world behaves as before). The server builds a Sign-In with Ethereum (EIP-4361) message bound to the page host, URI, the first configured chain, a 64-character hex nonce and a 60-second expiry, and verifies the signature against the message it stored (EOA only; smart-account EIP-1271/6492 signatures are refused for now). Verification is per connection, is not stored, is not sent to other players and does not change rank: builder access is still granted by the Solana token only. Solana and EVM keep separate sessions, cooldowns and attempt counters, so neither cancels the other. The wallet panel gets Solana / EVM tabs when both are enabled (the choice is remembered with the open/closed state); the EVM tab lists the announced wallets and shows the verified address. `viem` 2.57.8 is a server-only dependency (the browser bundle does not include it).
- client: lip sync. A remote player's voice loudness opens their avatar's mouth (the first VRM mouth preset among aa/oh/ou/ih/ee), measured on a listen-only WebAudio branch beside playback and smoothed (60 ms attack, 120 ms release). Mouths close for moderator mute, a disabled voice, a suspended AudioContext or beyond 20 m. Each avatar instance writes its own clone's morphs, so players sharing an avatar never mirror each other. Settings › Audio › Lip Sync, on by default on desktop and off on touch devices. Nothing is sent over the network; protocol and server are unchanged.
- tests: token gate E2E uses signed mock wallets and SPL account RPC responses to check local door collisions, wallet/admin and builder flows, outage recovery, and RPC credential isolation in Chromium/WebKit (Firefox advisory).
- apps: client-local `player.getTokenBalance()` and the protected `wallet` event expose verified COO balances to world scripts; reads are cached and local gates affect only the player’s own screen.
- server: per-socket wallet verification, signature-free builder rechecks, and one shared balance service with fresh-only grants and bounded requests.
- tests: Phase 3b-1 real-PhysX availability regressions and server/browser E2E probes cover contact mutation, deferred removal and repeated static-body calls, with independent falling-body and tick checks.
- tests: Phase 3 physics boundary probes and a descriptor-preserving native sentinel run on the server and browser (Chromium/WebKit required; Firefox advisory). A base-compatible `--sentinel-only --expect-violations` control proves detection on `ea5e8f0`; see the e2e README.
- core: `MONITOR_TICK=1` logs a per-minute summary of server tick times (mean/median/p90/max). (An earlier wording said the last window was also on `/status`; it never was.)
- ci: an end-to-end Playwright job (`test/e2e/`, Chromium/WebKit/Firefox) runs on pull requests and on demand
- server: upload rate limits and quotas (`UPLOAD_*` envs): per-user and global concurrency, per-minute rates and daily byte quotas per user and per address, a shared daily budget for users without a stored or wallet-verified builder rank (wallet-granted builders are limited per wallet, and a slot of the total is kept for stored-rank users), a transfer deadline per admitted upload, and a free-space floor for local assets. Refusals answer `429`/`503`/`507` with `Retry-After`. Compressed upload bodies are refused (`415`); overlapping uploads of the same content are stored and charged once.
- client: uploads run one at a time per tab, retry once on a short `Retry-After`, time out, and a failed `.hyp` import cancels its remaining uploads.
- server: connection limits for `/ws`: per address, a burst of 20, a sustained rate of 10/min and 8 concurrent connections (`WS_CONNECT_*` envs); IPv6 parents have `WS_CONNECT_CONCURRENT_PER_56=16` and `WS_CONNECT_CONCURRENT_PER_48=32`, and `WS_MAX_SOCKETS=64` caps all transports until TCP closes, including closing sockets. Refused upgrades receive HTTP 503 with `Retry-After: 10` before any database lookup. Raising or disabling `WS_MAX_SOCKETS` raises or removes the default 64 MiB total write-backlog bound.
- tests: who may send what, asked of the real server, and whether a server secret reaches a page (P14). Until now a rank check was tested through a harness that calls one handler at a time; nothing connected to a running server as a visitor and tried.
  - **A rule for every packet.** `test/lib/packetAccess.mjs` gives each of the 41 packet names the rule the server applies to a client that sends it (any connection, builder, admin, an admin-only setting, a rank above the target's, the holder of a move or an upload, the admin code, a wallet flow of a world that has the feature, a relay that is dropped, a name the server has no handler for, refused for everyone, an app's line of chat, or dependent on the world's settings). `test/packetAccess.test.js` fails when a name has no rule and when the table and `ServerNetwork`'s handlers disagree about which names are handled (the one check read from source text: the inventory of handlers).
  - **The handlers, one rule at a time.** `test/packetRefusals.test.js` drives `ServerNetwork`'s handlers through the network harness with joined players: the packet from a connection below the rank, the state it leaves, the refusal's log line, then the same packet from a connection with the rank. It covers each builder handler (called directly, so that its own check is asked and not only the inbound gate before it, which is asked separately), a visitor's setting refused before its content is read, the holder of a move or an upload, `/spawn`, `/chat clear` and `/server stats`, `modifyRank`, the admin-only settings, kick and mute against the target's rank, `/admin` up to the closed socket, the relays, the internal event names, another player, a player's own fields that only the server sets, and a player sent as `entityAdded`. A check removed from a handler fails there, whatever its variables are called.
  - **The wallet flows, which check where.** All eleven wallet packets are sent in the unit test in a world without their feature, with gating modules that record any read but of the switch and collaborators (the balance service, the RPC-backed services, the database, the world's events) that fail the test if touched, and again in a world with the feature, where the same state is acted on. Six of them are not answered to a sender without a wallet state of its own (a sign-in under way, a verified wallet) whatever the world, so their feature check cannot be seen from outside: `requestBuilderByToken`, `requestBuilderRecheck`, `revokeBuilderByToken`, `requestEvmVerify`, `revokeEvmWallet`, `setEvmTipReceive` are checked in the unit test only. The other five (`requestBuilderNonce`, `tokenBalanceRequest`, `requestEvmNonce`, `requestEvmTipIntent`, `evmTip`) are also sent by the suite to a server without the features, where nothing answers, and to one with them, where each is answered.
  - **The suite.** `test/e2e/access-control.mjs` (no browser; one step of the `ci` job after the server build) connects a visitor, a builder and an admin over raw sockets. For each rule a connection below the rank sends a well-formed packet; it then waits for the answer to a ping sent after it, looks for the server's log line where the server writes one, and reads the state from the join snapshot of a connection that joins afterwards. Where there is one, the same payload from a connection with the rank follows and has to take effect; the cases without such a control say so in their result line, with the reason (another player's fields, the fields of a player's own record that only the server sets, a player added through `entityAdded`, a player's removal and the internal event names are refused for an admin too; the server-to-client names have no handler for anyone). Also in it: a wrong `/admin` code up to the closed socket, a session token with a forged signature and one for a user that does not exist. For `appChat` it asserts what the server guarantees (a relayed line is an app line with no player id, never the system's or a tip's kind, and names the sending visitor in `via` whatever the packet claimed, also in a newcomer's snapshot, while a server script's line names nobody; none for an app that does not exist; a connection's lines are limited). What it cannot guarantee is not asserted: a line under the name of an app whose script did not send it is relayed (see "What remains" below), naming its sender.
  - **Secrets.** The suite starts the server with a sentinel in every variable classed as a secret (`JWT_SECRET`, `JWT_SECRET_PREVIOUS`, `ADMIN_CODE`, `AI_API_KEY`, `LIVEKIT_API_SECRET`, `SOLANA_RPC_URL`, `PUBLIC_RPC_URL`, `EVM_RPC_URL_137`, `ASSETS_S3_URI`; not `DB_URI`, which has to stay `local`) and searches `/`, `/env.js`, `/status`, `/health`, the refusals of routes (a page and an asset that do not exist, `/api/upload` and `/api/upload-check` with no token, a forged one and a malformed request, `/csp-report` with a report and three it refuses, `/ws` without an upgrade), a visitor's and an admin's join snapshot, and, once every connection is closed and the server has stopped, every packet received, the close frame of every connection the run made (an outdated client's, with its reason, included) and the server's whole log, bodies and headers, also inside the tokens found there, two levels deep (JWT segments, base64 and base64url, percent escapes, JSON escapes, the strings of whatever parses as JSON): every decoding is searched, text or not. The search is first tried on a sentinel planted in each of those encodings, also in JSON whose message is not ASCII. A second server with the wallet features on is searched the same way. `LIVEKIT_API_KEY` is in every voice token by design and is classed as public.
  - **Environment names.** `test/lib/envTable.mjs` classes every variable the server, the scripts and the agent read (secret, public, operator setting, test knob, set by the platform or the build); `test/envTable.test.js` fails for a name that is read and has no class, and for a class nothing reads.
  - **What these do not show.** The suite sends the payloads written in it: a packet shape nobody thought of is not tried. The secret search covers the surfaces listed and the encodings it decodes, and not the page's built scripts: they are built before the suite sets its sentinels, so they cannot hold them (`test/buildDefines.test.js` tests what a build writes into them). Four of the sentinels are in settings the run never uses (`SOLANA_RPC_URL`, `PUBLIC_RPC_URL`, `EVM_RPC_URL_137`, `ASSETS_S3_URI`): for them the search shows only that an unused setting is not dumped anywhere it looks, and nothing about the code paths that use them. The table of names is a list: it does not follow a value through the code.

### Changed
- dev: code that nothing used is removed (P18-6a), 86 lines, and one comment that named a part of it. No runtime behaviour changed.
  - **Removed.** `src/client/components/useElemSize.js`, a hook no file imports (22 lines); four icons of `src/client/components/Icons.js`, `ChatIcon`, `KeyboardIcon`, `CircleUpIcon` and `SettingsIcon` (44 lines); `strokeRoundRect` in `src/core/extras/roundRect.js` (8 lines); `propToCode` in `src/core/extras/buttons.js` (2 lines); `toBool` in `src/core/extras/scriptText.js` (5 lines); `isEvmConfigured` in `src/client/evm-integration.js` (2 lines); `isSolanaConfigured` in `src/client/solana-integration.js` (3 lines). And a comment: the todo in `src/core/nodes/UIView.js` that proposed `strokeRoundRect` for the border (1 line), which would otherwise name a function that no longer exists.
  - **Found dead by.** Each name appears nowhere else in the repository (`src/`, `test/`, `scripts/`, `docs/`, `examples/`, `agent.mjs`) but as history: this file's earlier entry on `useElemSize.js`, and an archived plan in `goals/archive/` on `propToCode`. No module imports one of these files as a namespace, and none is a build's entry point.
  - **Checked: the bundles.** The six bundles the repository builds (the page with its chunks, the particles worker, the server, the node client, the viewer and the world-client library) were built before and after with the repository's own build scripts, in exported copies of the tree, and compared file by file. Byte for byte the same: the server bundle's code, and the particles worker and the page's four chunks with their source maps. (The server's source map is not the same file: it holds the sources' text, `scriptText.js` without `toBool` among them; see the source maps below.) The node client, the viewer and the world-client library each lose one statement, the unused `var propToCode = …` (101, 101 and 103 bytes), and nothing else of their code. The page's minified entry is 71 bytes smaller: it loses that statement (63 bytes); two declarations are joined with a comma where `isSolanaConfigured` and `isEvmConfigured` stood (4 bytes each; tree shaking had already left both out); and the minifier gave 1,022 of its local names to other bindings (in 3,426 places). That renaming is one to one: a scope analysis finds every identifier bound as before (480,975 identifiers, 65,470 bindings), every other token is the same, and the same build with identifier minification off differs by the statement and the two joined declarations only. The order of the modules is the same in every bundle; the source maps of the server, the node client, the viewer and the world-client library differ by the removed lines, every mapping moved with its line.
  - **Not run.** No end-to-end suite was run locally for this change.
- tooling: the two parsers the tests import are declared. `test/serverWrites.test.js`, `test/wsAdmission.test.js` and `test/lib/networkSource.mjs` import `@babel/parser`, and `test/lib/gltfAnnotations.mjs` imports `acorn`; both were in the lock file only as packages that other development packages bring along, so an update of those could have removed them. They are now `devDependencies` at the versions already locked (`@babel/parser` 7.29.11, `acorn` 8.14.0). Nothing is installed that was not installed before, and the production install is unchanged.
- docs: four items, as the owner decided: three known oddities are left as they are and described where a script's author reads, each run or measured before it was written, and the code of conduct names this repository's contact.
  - **`prng`** (`docs/scripting/Utils.md`, which did not list it): the same seed gives the same numbers (Node, Chromium and WebKit), but they are not evenly spread (seed 1, `rand(0, 3)`, first 4,000 calls: 1441 / 737 / 1134 / 688), a range of 2^31 or more gives results below `min`, a seed other than 0 whose lowest 31 bits are 0 gives the same, smallest value every time, it is not for anything fair or secret, and `Math.random()` or a generator carried in the script (both run in a script) are what to use instead; it is not corrected because that would change every seed's numbers, and `num` is said to give its two ends half as often.
  - **A video with `visible: false`** (`Video.md`) loads its source, but it never reports `loading: false` or calls `onLoad`, and a `play()` made before it has its player stays pending, not carried out until the node is made visible; `duration` shows when its first frame is there, and a hidden video with a `linked` key loads the element that a shown video with the same `src` and key then uses (`loading`, `duration` and `onLoad` are described too).
  - **A mesh without a geometry** (`mesh/Mesh.md`): `app.create('mesh')` (`type: 'box'`, 1 × 1 × 1) draws nothing, whatever its type or size, until a geometry is set; once it has one it is drawn white (see Fixed for the crash that was there), and a prim or a clone of a model's mesh is what to use for a shape.
  - **`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`** sent reports about this fork's community to upstream's address; they go to this repository's maintainers now, by e-mail to the address the owner gave or through GitHub (the issue tracker for what can be public, the private reporting form for what cannot).
- docs: the node pages' defaults compared with the defaults the golden files record (row f of `test/golden/nodes/`), and the ones that disagreed corrected: video `doubleside` `false` and `height` `1` (its `width` and `height` are worked out from `aspect`, which the page called `.ratio`, and the page's `linked` is a boolean or a string), image `height` `1` and `doubleside` `false`, ui `doubleside` `true`, controller `radius` `0.4`, uiview `width` and `height` `null` and `flexBasis` / `flexGrow` / `flexShrink` `'auto'` / `0` / `1`, uiimage `backgroundColor` `null` (its text was `borderRadius`'s, which now has its own entry); prim `transparent` is described as the deprecated no-op it is, and the material page links to the mesh page where it is.
- docs: the top-level documents describe this repository instead of upstream's (P14).
  - **Rewritten.** `DOCKER.md` from the Dockerfile and the server's start-up checks (what the image holds and runs, the health check fixed to port 3000, the settings needed to start, where the world's data is; no `VOLUME`, no `USER`; its `docker` commands were not run). `CONTRIBUTING.md` for this fork (Node version, `npm run check`, `npm test`, the four builds, the e2e suites, pull requests to this repository). `MIGRATIONS.md` points to this file's `[Unreleased]` section and keeps upstream's 0.15.0 note as history. `goals/README.md` lists the design documents and the archive.
  - **README.** `npm run world:clean` is gone from it (there is no such script: clean-up runs at start-up with `CLEAN=true`); `npm run world:backup` is described as it is (what the archive holds, that it stays on the world's disk, the room it needs, PostgreSQL not included); upstream's hosted pages are named as upstream's; the Node version, the client-address header on Fly (`FLY_APP_NAME`), the `PUBLIC_` exception and the project status say what the code does.
  - **Moved.** `REFACTOR_PLAN.md`, `goals/_CONTEXT.md` and the nineteen `goals/phase0-*` to `phase5-*` task files to `goals/archive/`, each with a first line that says it is historical and from when. The `*-design.md` files stay where the source names them.
  - **Added.** `.env.example` has entries of their own for `NODE_ENV`, `MONITOR_TICK` and `TOKEN_BALANCE_TTL_SECONDS` (the first two were mentioned in passing elsewhere, the third nowhere).
  - **Tests.** `test/errorHandler.test.js`: the test that is skipped when run as root says why, and that skip is a failure when `CI` is set.
  - **README statements that claimed more than the code does.** A full server or an address at its limits refuses an admin like anyone else (the README said an admin can always come in: that holds for the join budget only). The free-space floor for uploads is a check at that moment, with nothing reserved, so uploads arriving together can cross it. A backup made while the server runs takes the files and the database at different moments. Also made exact: where assets are served from with S3, what the file-signature check looks at, which `eth_call` checks a contract wallet's signature, and that a grant is not looked at again when a balance changes.
  - **Corrected.** This file said `MONITOR_TICK=1` also put its last window on `/status`; it only logs. The CI workflow and `test/e2e/README.md` said every build empties `build/`; `client:build` and `viewer:build` do. `package.json`'s `homepage` pointed at upstream's repository and its `bugs` at upstream's issue tracker; the homepage is this repository's, and there is no `bugs` entry (issues are not enabled here).
- server: AI actions have a budget (`src/server/aiBudget.js`; see Security). A builder runs one action (create, edit or fix) at a time and 20 an hour; the world runs 2 at once and 60 an hour (`AI_MAX_PER_USER_PER_HOUR`, `AI_MAX_PER_HOUR`, `AI_MAX_CONCURRENT`). These numbers count admitted actions, not provider requests: a create is two requests (its script and its name, asked for together; its place is held until both have ended), an edit or a fix one, and the retries the OpenAI and Anthropic SDKs make on their own (two by default) are not counted.
  - **Deadline.** An action runs for at most 10 minutes in all (`AI_MAX_SECONDS`, from 1 to 86,400): reading the script, the provider requests with their retries, the name. Then its provider requests are aborted, its place is given back, and a reply that comes later changes nothing. (The SDKs' own timeouts end once a reply's headers have arrived; a reply that never finishes would have held its place for good.)
  - **Output limits.** Every provider client sets one: 16,000 tokens for a script, 4,096 for a name (Anthropic `max_tokens` and OpenAI `max_output_tokens`, both counting the model's thinking; xAI `max_completion_tokens`, which does not count its reasoning; Google `generationConfig.maxOutputTokens`, whose reference does not say). Before, only the Anthropic client set one. A reply cut off at the limit is an error, not a script.
  - **Refusals.** A request over a limit is not queued: the builder is told in chat. A value of an `AI_MAX_*` setting outside its range stops the server at start-up. The budget remembers at most 4,096 builders; when every one of them started an action within the hour or still runs one (which takes `AI_MAX_PER_HOUR` near that, or `AI_MAX_CONCURRENT` and `AI_MAX_SECONDS` raised so far that thousands of actions run for hours), a builder it does not know yet is told the AI is busy, and the others keep their counts.
  - **Placeholders.** `/create` places a "generating…" app before it asks. A create that is refused, fails, runs out of time or makes a script the world has no room for now removes that app, for everyone, also when its builder has left (the server does it; its blueprint stays, unused, as there is no way to remove one). `/create` without a description no longer places one.
  - A request is counted against the builder's user, or against the wallet of a builder who is one only by holding a token. The request itself is read into plain values: a prompt or an error text of at most 16,000 characters (longer is cut); of a crashed script's error, its `name`, `message`, `stack`, `fileName`, `code`, line and column and one level of `cause`, none of them required (a thrown `''` or `{ code }` can be fixed again; an error that says nothing is described to the provider by a fixed text).
- tests: the tests of the network system run the real `ServerNetwork` (P16, nine pull requests). They did not import `src/core/systems/ServerNetwork.js`: `test/lib/networkHarness.mjs` cut 90 of its methods out of the file's text with a regular expression and ran them in a `vm` context of their own, with about 80 names injected in place of the file's imports, and `test/lib/persistenceHarness.mjs` ran the whole text with its `import` lines taken out. Moving one method to another file broke them, which stood in the way of splitting the class, and the class ran in another realm than on the server, with stand-ins for its imports.
  - **The new helper.** `test/lib/network.mjs`: `loadNetwork()` bundles the class with its real imports (`test/lib/serverBundle.mjs`, esbuild) and imports it into the test's own realm; `makeNetwork(t, …)` makes a network through the real constructor and `init()` on a stub world, and the test's end stops it, so none of its timers outlives the test. The classes a test constructs (`Socket`, `RpcError`, `Settings`, …) come from the same bundle. A module can be replaced for a test: by test source, or the real one with some exports overridden (a setter changes them between calls). One build per test file and set of replacements (about 20 ms here, 260 ms for the first with its packages). A worker or a child process imports the same evaluation with `importNetwork({ url, env })`; `shutdownSave.test.js` builds the one its children use and removes it. `log` takes the lines of the console the constructor wires (its own once-per-subject lines), not components made after it. Its own tests are `test/networkHelper.test.js`.
  - **A known environment.** What the shell running the tests sets (`SAVE_INTERVAL=0`, a token mint, `WS_MAX_SOCKETS`, `ADMIN_CODE`, `ASSETS_BASE_URL`, …) no longer reaches these tests: every name the server reads (`test/lib/envTable.mjs`) is unset, as the old harness gave the class, while the bundle is evaluated and from `makeNetwork` to the test's end, when the values found are put back; a test's own values go on top. Bundles are evaluated one at a time across the process, each with the environment asked for from its first evaluation on. Only the files that ran against `REAL_PHYSX_SOURCE_ROOT` before read it. A test runs the ported files with such values set.
  - **Moved to it.** `persistence`, `persistedHydration`, `persistenceIntegration` (its worker, under lockdown, imports the bundle), `serverFileRead`, `shutdownSave` (through `test/lib/shutdownChild.mjs`), `announceBeforeApply`, `sharedObjects`, `scriptChat` and `blueprintEnforcement`: their set-up only, the same 111 tests, no assertion changed by the move. `persistenceHarness.mjs` no longer reads `ServerNetwork.js`.
  - **Four tests that could not fail.** Found by mutants that neither the old nor the moved files caught, and changed on purpose: concurrent saves (`persistence`) are checked after the turns the second save takes, not one microtask later; the fatal deadline test (`persistence`) has a second row behind the held write and waits the turns the row writer yields; the SQLite recovery test (`persistenceIntegration`) asserts that an acknowledged edit is already recorded when it is broadcast; a new `blueprintEnforcement` test sends a valid add under a stored id (the only one there was refused by the policy first). Of 50 mutants of the product code these files guard, the moved files catch all 50; the files as they were catch 44: not those four, and not a constructor that makes no inbound gate or whose `logOnce` writes nowhere (the old harness never ran the constructor).
  - **Moved in the second.** `test/lib/joinFixture.mjs`, every file that uses it (`actionBudgetHandlers`, `adminCommand`, `connectionHolds`, `eventBudget`, `externalAvatarUrls`, `joinAdmission`, `joinBudget`, `moderation`, `packetRefusals`, `playerCoalescing`, and the one test of `packetAccess` that does), and `aiBudget`, `networkTransforms`, `userRowsNetwork` and `writeLedger`: the same 216 tests. Their set-up changed: a test hands the fixture its context, and what the constructor or `init()` sets is no longer set by hand (the relay drop of `packetRefusals`, the databases). The stand-in database of `networkTransforms` answers the read of the user ids at start-up, which the old harness's network failed and logged elsewhere; the wallet test of `packetRefusals` gives the network back the collaborators it booby-trapped once its checks are done, since the test's end now stops the network. The fixture's app and player are in `test/lib/joinEntities.mjs`. As under the old harness, these networks save nothing on a timer (`SAVE_INTERVAL` 0).
  - **Modules a test gives.** What the old harness injected for a join (`readJWT`, `createJWT`, `expiredSessionUser`, `uuid`) and the gating modules a test gave the class (`context: { solanaGating, evmGating }`) are stand-in modules: `loadNetwork({ standIns })` makes a module a CommonJS one (`replace` takes `{ commonjs }`) whose every export is read, each time the code uses it, from the object `makeNetwork(t, { modules })` gave for the test, and from the real module for a name the object lacks; a `Proxy` given there sees each read that is left in the compiled bundle, as `packetRefusals` needs to show that a handler reads nothing of a module before its switch (esbuild removes a read of a module's export whose value is unused, a bare `void solanaGating.WALLET_HOST`, as the server's own build does; a read of a property of an exported object stays). A stand-in is the module's, for the whole bundle, where the old harness gave the names to the class's methods one by one: `onConnection`'s session tokens and ids are now also those of the other methods (the join snapshot's fixed part is measured with the fixture's token), and `evm-gating.js` reads a `solana-gating.js` a test gave; no test relied on either not doing so. `entityClasses(url)` gives `Entities` the classes a file of the tests exports.
  - **One end for all a test made.** Every network a test made is stopped in one hook at its end, each whatever another one's stop threw, and the environment and the stand-ins' objects are put back there. node:test runs no hook of a test after one that threw: with a hook per network, a stop that failed (a booby-trapped collaborator) left the later networks' socket checks running, and the test file did not end. The timers the class keeps in its fields (the socket check, a save timer, a tip announcement) are cleared even where a stop threw part way; a collaborator's own (a tip watcher whose stop threw) are not. The hook fails with what a stop threw, a falsy value included. That state is the process's, so one test holds the helper at a time: a `makeNetwork` of another test meanwhile throws (tests that make networks must not run concurrently).
  - **Three expectations corrected, toward what the server does.** Run in the test's own realm, a user's row gets fewer statements than the old harness showed. `src/server/userRows.js` keeps one writer per user, and a value decided while a statement is under way goes out with the next statement (one replaced before its turn is not written). Under the old harness each `await` of a handler's promise, which came from the `vm`'s realm, took extra microtask turns, enough for every statement to land before the next change. The counts the tests now see (`adminCommand`, four `/admin` toggles: 3 statements; `actionBudgetHandlers`, ten rank changes: 6) are what these tests' immediate mocks give, not what the server promises, so the tests assert what it does promise: once the writer is idle (`userRows().idle()`), the last statement and the row hold the value decided last and nothing decided is unwritten (`unstored()` is 0), with between 2 statements (the first value and the last) and one per change; a writer that wrote every change at once would be within those bounds, and is. `actionBudgetHandlers`' test that a change over the identity budget writes nothing takes its count once the writer is idle (taken at once, it missed the last statement of the five changes before, which the test then charged to the refused sixth). The merging itself is shown in one new test of each file: the first statement is held, three more values are decided meanwhile, and once it is released, with no further change, exactly one more statement writes the last of them. No write is lost.
  - **A re-key's collision loop.** A new `joinBudget` test gives the ids a moved app draws: one an app in the world has, one a known user has, then a free one; the app moves to the third and the other two records are as they were. Nothing tried the loop before (generated ids do not collide).
  - **Mutants.** Of 118 mutants of the product code these files guard, the moved files catch 110 and the files as they were 109. Three are caught only now: a rank stored as the one before the change and a user row that keeps a column's first decision (the state the corrected tests assert), and a constructor whose relay drop writes nowhere (`packetRefusals` made its own). Two were caught only before: a bare `void` read of a gating module ahead of a handler's switch, which esbuild drops from a bundle as having no effect (the server's own build included); the same reads written so that they stay in the bundle are caught by both.
  - **Moved in the third.** `serverWallet` (the token gate), `serverEvmWallet` (EVM sign-in, holdings and tips) and `connectionLifecycle` (the join, the takeover of a silent connection, a close during each wait): the same 139 tests, no assertion changed. What the old harness injected into the class, and into the `onConnection` it cut on its own (`connectionMethod()`, now removed), is given through stand-ins: the gating modules (the bundle's own, with their switches set), the session tokens and a new user's id, the makers of the token service (`solanaRpc.js`, `tokenBalance.js`) and `createEvmRpcs` (`evmRpc.js`; `RpcError` stays the bundle's). `connectionLifecycle` runs the class's own `onDisconnect` (it cut a copy of its own before) and still takes the route from `loadRoute`. The tests' `walletOf` and the limits they read (`evm.TIP_WATCH_MAX`) are the bundle's modules now, evaluated in the known environment: with `WS_MAX_SOCKETS=7` in the shell, `serverEvmWallet` failed before (the `evm-gating.js` it imported and gave the class had a watch table of 14 places, and a test fills 18), and it passes now.
  - **The class's timers.** `makeNetwork(t, { timers })`: the class's own calls of `setTimeout`, `clearTimeout`, `setInterval` and `clearInterval` go to the test's object (a name it lacks: the global function), as the old harness injected them, so `serverEvmWallet`'s fake timers still count the tip announcement that waits. The bundle imports `test/lib/networkTimers.mjs` on the first line of `ServerNetwork.js` (`serverBundle.mjs`'s new `prepend`), so only that file's calls go there: the modules it imports (a tip watcher, the admin code's brake) call the globals. The object is set before the constructor runs and put back at the test's end, after its networks are stopped.
  - **Six checks no test could fail.** Of the mutants these three files guard, six were caught by neither the old files nor the moved ones nor any other file; five new tests catch them: a Solana challenge message with another domain than the world's, and a Solana answer from a second wallet that signs the challenge and gives its own address (one test, `serverWallet`); a tip intent to a verified player who has not opted in, a watched tip's result after a fatal stop, an announced tip told to its recipient privately as well, and a holdings read kept across a new sign-in of the same address (`serverEvmWallet`).
  - **Mutants.** 171 mutants of the product code these files guard (101 from the earlier mutation scripts, 70 new: the nonce and its expiry, the address and the message of a challenge, the switches, the cooldowns, a revoke, the holding check, a tip's way from intent to announcement, and the join's waits, takeover, limit and kick list). The moved files catch 145 and the files as they were the same 145; with the five new tests, 151. The other twenty are caught by another file: sixteen (the place a join holds, what a departed connection held) by `connectionHolds`, for which they were written as well, two (the kick list) by `moderation`, a Solana nonce's expiry by `solanaGating` and the address an EVM answer is compared with by `evmGating`. The timers and `prepend` are caught when they are broken, by the helper's tests and by `serverEvmWallet`.
  - **After the review: a seventh check, and the helper's end.** A Solana challenge's expiry was tried only on `checkChallenge` itself (`solanaGating`): with the handler handing it a clock of `0` in place of `Date.now()`, every wallet and gating test passed. A new `serverWallet` test answers a challenge one millisecond before its end (verified, granted) and another at its end, with a right signature (refused, no balance read, no grant). And when a network's stop threw half way, the helper cancelled what was left with the global `clearTimeout`, which does nothing to a handle of the timers a test gave: a tip announcement waiting on them stayed. It is now cancelled with the test's own functions first (a new `networkHelper` test, which also has one of them throw: the stop's error is the one reported, and the helper is free for the next test). And a test may give two networks an object of timers each (`serverEvmWallet`'s restart does): there is one table for the process, which then holds the second, so the first network's timers were cancelled with the second's functions and stayed. The shim now remembers which object made a timer and cancels it with that one; a handle it did not make, or one that is not an object, goes to the object in the table as before.
  - **Moved in the fourth.** `protocolVersion`, `wsAdmission` and `wsTransport` hand the `/ws` route a network of the real class (`wsTransport`'s child processes import the one the test builds, as `shutdownSave`'s do; they are started as before, with its `{ url, env }` as one more argument). The route itself is still cut out of `src/server/index.js`'s text, now by `test/lib/wsRoute.mjs`, from which `connectionLifecycle` takes it too, until it moves into a module of its own. The child does not start the class's socket check, which pings every connection every 10 s: the old harness never ran it, its pings would be data on the raw connections the cases count, and the longest case (`pin`) takes about 7 s here. `inboundNetwork`, `networkLogging` and `physicsFailure` ran methods cut out of `ServerNetwork.js` by regular expressions of their own; they run the real class (`physicsFailure` against `REAL_PHYSX_SOURCE_ROOT` when it is set, as before: a baseline whose `stopForFatal` does not stop fails it). What the cut class was given is given as in the earlier steps: an admin code in the environment, and the token gate's switch through a stand-in of its module (`networkLogging`). `networkHelper`'s run of the ported files with a hostile environment includes these six. The same 53 tests; no expectation changed by the move, but `networkLogging` now states one the old harness had without saying so, and `physicsFailure` one it relied on (both below).
  - **Set-up that differs.** `inboundNetwork`'s inbound gate is the constructor's, whose clock is the real one (the old test's stood at 1,000 ms; a clock that moves only refills the gate's budget, and no test of the file asks for a refusal by rate). The class `networkLogging` cut out had a console with nothing but `error`, so a line on `console.warn`, `log` or `info` from a handler failed the test; the test now fails on a line written to any other method of the console (its stand-in is built from the console's own keys, so a method added to it later is covered as well). `wsAdmission`'s test of the address handed on gave the route a plain object as the network; it gives it a real one whose `onConnection` it takes over.
  - **The fatal stop's test, said outright.** `physicsFailure` called each handler of a network that had stopped, without arguments, and asked only that none threw. The class it cut out had no world, no gating module, no inbound gate and no log, so a handler that went past its stop check threw there. On the real class, 13 of 24 mutants of the stop passed that test, 11 of which the old one caught (a handler's check removed, or reduced to `this.world.fatalError`, and the flush's). It now fails on anything a handler touches after the stop: the connection, payload and meta it is called with, the world, the token gate's module (a stand-in that reads as the real module until the stop) and a line of the network's log. With that it catches the same 22 of the 24 as before, and the other two once `onEntityRemoved` and `onEntityEvent` are given an id and an event (below): they checked the payload's type first, and returned on a wrong one whether or not they had checked the stop.
  - **Read as text, on purpose.** `packetAccess` keeps reading `ServerNetwork.js` for which `on<Name>` members the class declares (a static inventory; nothing is run from that text, and a comment says so); whether a packet reaches its method is asked of the real class. `imageCheck`'s stop-path tripwire finds `installShutdown`, `flushBeforeExit`, `saveDirty`, `saveConfig` and `userRows` by parsing (`test/lib/networkSource.mjs`, `@babel/parser`), the network's in the class and in any module under `src/server/network/` (where parts of the class are to move; the folder does not exist yet), every definition of the name, instead of by their signature text in `ServerNetwork.js`, and reads their log calls from the syntax tree as well: a call of a member named `error`, `warn`, `log`, `info`, `debug`, `trace` or `logOnce`, of any object, a call of a name the file binds to such a member, and either of them called through `.call`, `.apply`, `Reflect.apply` or a `.bind(…)` called at once; and in those functions a logger may only be called at once by a written name: `console`, a `{ log = console }` option of the function or of a function in it, written so (`installShutdown`'s; not renamed, `{ log: audit = console }`, nor under another name; a logger inside the function whose parameter it is), and a member named `error`, `warn`, `log`, `info`, `debug`, `trace` or `logOnce` of any other object (`this.logOnce`, `audit.error`). Any other use (kept in a name, destructured by a written, string or template key, passed, called through `.call`, `.apply`, `.bind` or `Reflect.apply`, a computed member) is a violation, which is not followed; no binding is looked up. The count of log calls and the violations ask one function which names are loggers where (`loggerScope`), so a direct call of a nested function's option is counted whatever its member (`log.table(…)`), and one helper gives the static name of a key, for members and destructured properties alike (`staticKey`). One shape is accepted for today's code: `userRows`' `onFailure: ({ userId, columns, error })`, whose `error` is a value handed to `briefError` (every use of the name in the function is an argument of a call), not a logger called there. (A regular expression did the second part before, and missed a space before the parenthesis, a renamed console, a destructured or bound logger, a computed key and the other levels.) Both tripwires parse with `@babel/parser`. The file and line in the names of its six tests of a log line are found the same way (today they are the numbers the names spelled by hand), and each of those tests fails when its function no longer logs that line. `serverWrites` lets the network's own receivers (`this`, `socket`, `tSocket`) send in the class and under `src/server/network/` only; before, it let them in any file named `ServerNetwork.js`. There a receiver, the whole expression a `send` or `ping` is called on (a call's result is not the name it is called by: `socket().send(…)` is refused), has to be what its name says: `this` that of a class member or of a module's top-level function (with the arrows inside them), not that of a nested non-arrow function nor the module's; and for a `socket` or `tSocket` that sends or pings, every binding or write of that name in its unit (the class member, field's function or module's top-level function that holds the call, nested functions included) has to be an origin on a list of exactly the shapes today's class binds its sent-to connections by. No use is matched to the binding it resolves to, so shadowing or a write in a nested function can only refuse more, and nothing on the right of a binding is followed. The list: a plain parameter of the unit (44 of today's 48 sends and pings, in 23 members); a plain parameter of a function passed directly to `this.sockets.forEach`, or to `forEach` of a local array that only receives that name from such a parameter, in `checkSockets`' shape exactly; a declaration or assignment of `this.sockets.get(…)` (`sendTo`, `announceTip`, `onKick`). Where an origin reads `this.sockets`, its `this` has to be the unit's own, and either name bound or written at a network module's top level is refused.
