A store that is already larger loads as it is and may shrink; a key stored before the key rule (longer than 128 characters, or empty) can still be overwritten without growing and deleted. `world.set(key, undefined)` deletes a key (it used to log an error and change nothing), and a value that cannot be turned into JSON throws (it used to be logged and dropped). `world.get` used to return the stored object itself, so one app could change what another read without calling `set`. Both are now documented in `docs/scripting/world/World.md`.
- scripts: `world.set` throws ("the server is stopping") once the final save on shutdown has started, so the server never acknowledges a value it will not write. When the stop begins, app timers are cleared and no app starts its script again: a build still waiting for its assets, a crash rebuild or a blueprint change does nothing from then on.
- server: an upload is refused (`507`) when free space cannot be read, instead of being let through.
- ci: the end-to-end job also runs on every push to `main` and weekly (Mondays 04:23 UTC), so `main` is checked even when a pull request skipped the suites through its paths filter.
- chat: a user's message always shows a name (`Anonymous` when empty), and names made only of spaces or invisible characters are refused on the server and the client, so a user message can no longer look like a system message.
- wallet: a Solana verification that times out after the verify request was sent is withdrawn on the server (`revokeBuilderByToken`), so a late success cannot leave the server verified (and a builder) behind an unverified panel.
- network: tip packets are appended (`setEvmTipReceive`, `requestEvmTipIntent`, `evmTipIntent`, ids 35-37) with inbound caps of 32 and 192 bytes.
- network: EVM wallet packets are appended to the packet table (`requestEvmNonce`, `evmNonce`, `requestEvmVerify`, `evmWalletStatus`, `revokeEvmWallet`, ids 30-34) with inbound caps of 128, 512 and 16 bytes for the client-sent ones. Existing ids and the protocol version (`v=2`) are unchanged, so existing node agents keep working.
- stage, prims: memory for prims and linked meshes stays bounded (Phase 3b-2b-2). A Model with no instances is released after 10 s of world time (its instanced mesh, geometry clone and BVH); the material it rendered with is kept per Model id, so scripts' material proxies and their edits survive visible toggles, LOD switches and rebuilds, and the uber-shader hook is installed once per material. An unlinked mesh disposes the material and textures it cloned. Prim geometry keys are finite and the same in every process: tapered cylinder radii snap to a 1/64 grid and torus tube ratios to a log2 grid of 1/8 octave (computed from the float bits, since Math.log2/Math.pow may differ by engine), and a ratio outside 2^-10..2^10 is invalid (no visual, no physics). Prim materials round metalness and roughness to 0.05, so untextured materials form a fixed palette (at most 21 × 21 × 21 × 2) that every process builds the same way; textures are first come, with at most 256 texture strings and 1024 textured materials per process, beyond which a prim renders untextured with one warning. Kept linked materials are capped at 8192 or the live Models, whichever is larger (oldest released first), and Stage.destroy disposes Models and materials. Mesh box and sphere geometries are shared by size and held while a mesh is mounted with them; at most 1024 unused ones per shape stay cached (least recently released first out, disposed when evicted), so the cache holds the sizes that mounted meshes use plus 1024 per shape (how many meshes an app mounts is not limited here). A geometry's id is its shape and size, so a linked mesh that was inactive while its geometry was evicted comes back to its Model and material edits, as long as the stage still keeps them (the Model for 10 s without instances, the material within the kept-material limit). An exception while a mesh is inserted into the stage takes the mesh or its instance back out instead of leaving it rendering (a non-finite matrix is parked by the octree, not refused, and still renders as before). Linked render flags count only when they are `true`. Values that reach a cache key must be primitives (a `Number`, `Boolean` or `String` object, whose own `toString` could mint keys, is refused): Mesh `width`, `height`, `depth` and `radius` (which also refuse `NaN` and ±Infinity now), Prim `metalness`, `roughness` and `opacity`, Prim `texture`, and Prim `doubleside`, `castShadow` and `receiveShadow`. A Prim keeps a plain copy of the size it is given (at most 16 values) and hands scripts a copy back, so a custom iterator, index getter or later edit cannot change the size after it was checked.
- physics: a Collider skipped for its scale, extent or pose, a Prim skipped for its size, scale, extent, position or mirror, and a RigidBody skipped for its position mount again once that transform changes (an extent skip keeps its cooked mesh, so the retry does not cook again; moves that leave the checked transform unchanged do not retry).
- physics: no native allocation outlives its node, and unchanged values do not rebuild (Phase 3b-2b-1). Per mount, RigidBody leaked one or two PhysX objects, Controller four, and every RigidBody and Joint node kept scratch objects forever; a visible Controller leaked its capsule geometry, BVH and material on clients. Assigning the same mass, damping, centre of mass, Collider size/material/flags, Controller size or Joint limit every frame rebuilt the node every frame, and a RigidBody rebuild silently dropped its joints for good. Joints now detach before their body's actor is released and mount again on the new one, wait (instead of pinning the other body to the world) while a body has no actor, and a joint's failure stays in its own app. A native throw mid-mount no longer leaks temporaries or publishes a stale physics handle. Meshes: the vertex count and every index are checked before anything is allocated (whole triangles, indices inside the vertices); each mesh may have 200 000 vertices and 200 000 triangles (convex hulls 50 000 points), each app 400 000 and the process 2 000 000 vertices plus triangles of live cooked meshes (the production world's largest mesh has about 10 000 triangles), so one app cannot exhaust the wasm heap, which only grows and aborts at 2 GB.
- server: refuses to start when `JWT_SECRET` is still the public template value `hyper` (the value `.env.example` shipped and upstream hyperfy ships): a session token only carries a userId, so anyone who knows the secret can sign in as any user, admins included. A secret shorter than 32 characters logs a warning. `.env.example` no longer ships a value and says how to generate one. Upgrade note: set your own `JWT_SECRET` (`openssl rand -hex 32`) before deploying; every existing session token then becomes invalid, so admins sign in again with `/admin <ADMIN_CODE>`.
- physics: values that PhysX treats as invalid never reach it (Phase 3b-2a). A builder script could hand PhysX a denormal mass, a far controller position, a zero kinematic quaternion or a mesh whose real vertices are far larger than its file claims, and the shared scene's other bodies turned NaN; an invalid cone or hinge limit, a zero joint frame, a far centre of mass or an extreme push turned the body itself or its jointed bodies NaN. Lengths (box half-extents, sphere radii, controller radius/height), mesh scale axes, mesh extents (from the scanned vertices, never the file's bounding box) and positions must be inside 1e-6…1e6; geometries must pass `PxGeometryQuery.isValid`; a node that fails skips its physics (the visual stays) with one warning per node and reason, and a skipped Collider no longer re-cooks on every move and mounts once a setter fixes the value. Setters reject NaN, ±Infinity and negative lengths, frictions and restitutions, and a positive mass below 1e-9 (0 stays PhysX's infinite mass); a zero radius or size is accepted and skipped at mount. `setQuaternion` and `setKinematicTarget` normalise their quaternion (a zero or denormal one is ignored). Pushes (`addForce`, `addForceAtPos`, `addForceAtLocalPos`, `addTorque`) share a budget per body and physics step: their summed velocity change, from float32-rounded operands and with the lever measured from the centre of mass, stays within 1e6 m/s and rad/s, and an application point outside the window is ignored. Every actor pose write stays inside the window (RigidBody mount, `setPosition`, `setKinematicTarget`, node moves, Prim mount, joint anchors); a controller ignores a teleport or move outside it and gets no controller when mounted outside it. A Collider's local pose is checked and normalised; a mirrored Prim keeps static physics only, since a moving one would flip its visual away from its collider. Joints normalise frame rotations (a zero one skips the joint), apply a cone only when PhysX accepts it, a hinge limit only when ordered and inside ±360°, each distance end only when set (PhysX's default max-distance flag at 0 is cleared first), a spring only when finite and ≥ 0, and treat a NaN or negative break force as unbreakable. Prim `layer` takes the same four names as Collider and Controller (and scripts cannot pick `player`), and the `Joint.type` setter compares and reports the value.
- deps: security refresh of the lockfile (`npm audit fix`, no `--force`) and `@fastify/static` 8 → 10. Notable: fastify 5.2.0 → 5.12.7, ses 1.10.0 → 1.15.0 (a Compartment can no longer read the host's top-level `const`/`let`/`class` bindings), @fastify/busboy 3.1.0 → 3.2.4 (multipart DoS fixes), jws 3.2.3, fast-uri 3.1.9, @fastify/proxy-addr in place of proxy-addr, and fast-xml-parser gone from the tree. The unused `@fastify/multipart` dependency is removed. `npm audit` drops from 56 advisories (2 critical, 22 high) to 10 (3 high), all in the Solana client libraries or the esbuild dev server and not reachable here. Static responses keep the same headers: `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0` as before, because send used to overwrite the values `setHeaders` set (static 10 lets them through, so those lines are removed).
- apps: reading a name that a script never defined now gives `undefined` instead of throwing a `ReferenceError` (ses 1.12 makes the scope proxy opaque). Assigning to one still throws. None of the production world's scripts reads an undefined name.
- server: non-canonical static paths (`//x`, `/./x`, `/a/../x`) are refused with 403, and `/%61ssets/…` is no longer served as `/assets/…`. The root error handler now keeps a 4xx status and the error's headers (for example 400 invalid JSON, 413, 415 for a Brotli body, 403, 416 with Content-Range) and logs one line without a stack or query string; other errors are still logged with their stack and answered 500. @fastify/static 10 also answers 403 when it cannot list a static directory (EIO, EMFILE), so a run of `[http] 403` lines on ordinary paths points to a storage fault. The server refuses to start when `ASSETS_BASE_URL` ends with `/`, since asset paths would become `//name`.
- server: server-side `fetch` never decodes Brotli. A wrapper around undici's global dispatcher (installed right after the SES lockdown, before any repo module runs) removes `br` and `*` from every request's Accept-Encoding and refuses any response whose Content-Encoding contains `br` before fetch creates a decoder, including redirect targets. This covers app scripts' `fetch`, remote models and scripts loaded by the server, GLTF subresources and the AI and RPC clients (CVE-2026-111497 defence in depth). A Brotli-only server now fails a server-side fetch; browsers are unaffected. An explicit `Content-Encoding: identity` request body is accepted again.
- server: request bodies are decompressed only from gzip and deflate; a `Content-Encoding: br` request is refused before any decoding. No client sends compressed bodies, and this keeps unauthenticated input away from the Brotli decoder (CVE-2026-111497 defence in depth; Node 22.23.4 already carries the fix). Responses are still Brotli-compressed. The Docker image upgrades the system `brotli-libs` (used by the healthcheck's curl) to 1.2.1.
- server: Node.js 22.11.0 → 22.23.4 (Docker image, `.nvmrc`, engines), picking up the October 2026 security release and the seven earlier 22.x security releases since 22.11.0. Relevant here: the Brotli decoder out-of-bounds write (CVE-2026-111497, reachable through server-side `app.fetch` of an attacker-controlled `br` response), the TLS hostname-verification bypasses for outbound HTTPS (CVE-2026-106069, CVE-2026-106072), keep-alive response confusion (CVE-2026-105481), and the undici, OpenSSL, c-ares and CA store updates. Same major and module ABI (127), so native modules are unchanged.
- network: wallet status and balance packets are appended to the packet table (`walletStatus`, `tokenBalanceRequest`, `tokenBalance`, `requestBuilderRecheck`); existing ids and the protocol version (`v=2`) are unchanged, so existing node agents keep working. Release B uses a Sign-In-With-Solana (SIWS) challenge with the configured domain/origin, a 64-character hex nonce, issued/60-second expiration timestamps and a 1024-byte cap; deploy the server and client wallet changes together. Tabs opened before the update cannot verify a wallet until they reload.
- client: the wallet panel separates verification, server-read COO balance and build permission, with a script-exposure notice. Connect signs the server message once; refresh needs no signature. Attempts cancel safely on disconnect and bound nonce/ack waits to 15 seconds. Browsers no longer receive `PUBLIC_RPC_URL`; operators should use server-only `SOLANA_RPC_URL` and rotate previously exposed keys.
- physics (Phase 3b-1): contact and trigger callbacks receive fresh event objects per recipient, including independent contact arrays and vectors; events are no longer shared or reused and remain valid when retained. Synthetic contact-end and trigger-leave events caused by removal run at the next physics step, before simulation; removed, remounted or rebuilt recipients are skipped. Native callbacks contain thrown values before returning to PhysX.
- physics (Phase 3b-1): a crash stops the owning app's script and timers immediately, with one deferred crash rebuild per run. Latched API calls report that the app has stopped; only a native trap uses the physics-failure message. During destroy dispatch, world/app reads, app.send/sendTo, world.off/app.off and world.remove of owned nodes remain available for cleanup; native queries, creation and other native-capable calls remain blocked. A separate, script-free simulation health probe checks PhysX after a trap. An unusable module or an interrupted main-scene step stops the world; the server refuses mutations, attempts a final save under one five-second deadline (including in-flight saves and pending changes to loaded settings/spawn), logs unsaved records and exits with code 1 for an on-failure process restart. Clients stop ticking and show a reload error. A successful probe does not certify geometry correctness.
- server: persisted blueprints use the network blueprint policy at startup, including stripping unknown fields. App transforms are repaired in memory where possible; unusable blueprint/app rows are skipped with a boot summary and remain untouched in the database. Repairs are saved only after an explicit edit. With `CLEAN=true`, all reference discovery (including persisted skipped rows, nested assets, settings and users) finishes before deletion; malformed discovery data skips all cleaning for that boot.
- physics (sandbox Phase 3): PhysX conversions are host-private and copy primitive numbers. Native wrappers no longer enter script-accessible THREE methods, and the late PhysX prototype installation is removed. Scripts that directly accessed `toPxVec3`, `toPxExtVec3`, `fromPxVec3` or `toPxTransform` (none known in production) now get `undefined`.
- apps: physics vector inputs and output targets accept both endowed and stock THREE instances; duck-typed lookalikes throw intrinsic errors. Non-finite/float32-overflow vectors and quaternions cause mutators to do nothing, raycasts to return `null`, overlaps to return `[]`, and velocity-at-position queries to return zero; each invalid-input case warns once. Native failures reached synchronously through node proxies are rethrown as intrinsic errors.
- apps: raycast distance accepts zero and Infinity; omitted/null distance means infinite. Query masks default to unfiltered `0` for omitted/null, otherwise accept integer signed/unsigned 32-bit masks. Overlap radii must remain finite and positive in float32. Scalar setters beyond these query arguments remain outside Phase 3.
- physics: captured Enhanced vector output setters notify dirty tracking once rather than the three callbacks from the old component-by-component helper. Node transforms still re-commit.
- network: shared player/app positions and spawn positions are bounded per component to `TRANSFORM_LIMIT = 1e6` metres (inclusive). Player movement axis/gaze components use the same bound. Quaternions with norm within 0.1 of 1 are normalised; other rotations are dropped. App scale components must remain finite and non-zero when converted to f32 (including `1e-9`; no `1e6` scale cap). Invalid stored spawn data falls back to the default; invalid spawn changes are refused. This transform hotfix makes no additional protocol change; out-of-range values from any client are dropped. Local movement can still leave the shared world extent, but observers retain the last in-range position.
- apps: teleport positions are limited to `1e6` per component and server-to-client push relays to `1e4` per component; non-finite/out-of-range packets are ignored. Local script teleport uses the same check; direct local script push bypasses the relay bound. Local effects consume only finite `snare` in `[0, 1]`, boolean `turn`, and a valid string emote URL; invalid values are ignored. Falsy `duration` values (`0`, `''`, `null`, `undefined`, `NaN` or `false`) mean no expiry; other values count down as `Number(duration) - delta`, storing positive remaining time or ending the effect otherwise. Negative or non-numeric truthy durations end on the next frame, numeric strings expire after that many seconds, and `Infinity` never expires. Network `anchorId` must be a string of at most 128 characters. Emote speed `s` must parse to a finite number in `[0.01, 100]`, otherwise it defaults to 1; `l`/`g` flags are unchanged.
- [BREAKING] network: the protocol is now `v=2`. Old tabs receive the `outdated` kick and must reload; node clients must be rebuilt (`npm run node-client:build`) and restarted.
- [BREAKING] apps: client `app.send` uses plain MessagePack: string-keyed Maps become objects, Sets become arrays, Errors become `[name, message, cause]`, RegExps become `[source, flags]`, functions become `undefined`, and numeric typed arrays other than `Uint8Array` lose their type and can change bytes. Dates and Maps with non-string keys are refused; Symbols, BigInts outside the signed/unsigned 64-bit range, BigInt typed arrays, cycles and objects with more than 65,535 keys throw in the sender. Repeated object references become independent copies and `-0` becomes `0`. See [app.send](docs/scripting/app/App.md#sendname-data-skipnetworkid) for the full behaviour table.
- apps: client `app.send` returns `false` when the packet does not fit its byte, node or format limits. Server `app.send` and `sendTo` return `false` when output exceeds the packet or script tick budget, or a recipient is too far behind; a broadcast can still reach other recipients. Script chat bodies must be text; numbers are converted and other values are refused. At high rates, send deltas rather than full state (about 32k decoded nodes/s per connection).
- client: a blueprint, including its props, must fit in a 64 KiB message. Oversized edits show a toast before local mutation or upload; client and server apply the same blueprint policy.
- server: IPv6 parent rate buckets now cap /56 churn at burst 40 and 20/min, and /48 churn at burst 80 and 40/min (`WS_CONNECT_RATE_PER_MIN_56`, `WS_CONNECT_BURST_56`, `WS_CONNECT_RATE_PER_MIN_48`, `WS_CONNECT_BURST_48`). Either value set to 0 disables that parent bucket. Refusals charge no buckets; addresses and parents share the bounded idle-purged table.
- server: world blueprints share a 512 KiB encoded budget (`WORLD_BLUEPRINT_BYTES`). Over-budget adds remove the optimistic entity; modifications receive a rate-limited revert. Existing worlds above budget load with a warning and can still shrink. Builders should keep large text out of props because the whole world must fit in about 1 MiB for joins.
- [BREAKING] apps: scripts now run under real SES hardening. The built-in prototypes (`Object.prototype`, `Array.prototype`, `Math`, ...) are frozen and every endowed global (`Vector3`, `console`, `num`, ...) is read-only: assigning to one throws a `TypeError` inside the offending script. Each script asset runs in its own compartment, so nothing a script puts on its `globalThis` is visible to another app.
- [BREAKING] apps: `fetch().blob()` returns a script-safe blob (`size`, `type`, `arrayBuffer()`, `text()`, `slice()`) instead of a DOM `Blob`: `stream()`/`bytes()` are gone and `instanceof Blob` is false. `URL.createObjectURL` accepts only these blobs and serves them with an allow-listed media type (images/audio/video/models) or `application/octet-stream`. `fetch()` options are limited to `method`, `headers`, `body`, `mode`, `credentials`, `cache` and `redirect`.
- [BREAKING] apps: `world.open()` opens `http(s)` URLs only, never `javascript:`/`data:`/`blob:` URLs or files from the world's `/assets/` store (percent-encoded spellings included); a scheme-less `host:port/path` is rejected as well (use `http://host:port/path`); new tabs are opened with `noopener,noreferrer`.
- apps: errors raised inside `world.*`/`app.*` methods, `world.load()` and response readers reach scripts as fresh built-in errors of the matching class (`instanceof TypeError` etc. still holds) carrying `name`, `message` and `stack` only: no `cause`, no extra properties, no custom-class identity.
- apps: `Math.random()` has always been available to scripts; the Utils docs now say so.
- core: a node whose transform update throws (eg a script put a non-number into its own transform) now crashes its app instead of stalling the whole world; non-app nodes are retried up to 10 times, then left alone until written again. Until the shared THREE prototypes are hardened (follow-up), a failure in one app may still be caused by another app's prototype patch.
- [BREAKING] apps: `player.teleport()`, `player.push()` and `player.setSessionAvatar()` act on another player from server scripts only. A client script calling one of them on a remote player does nothing (logged once per app, player and method), and the server no longer relays these packets between clients; a client that still sends one has it dropped (logged once per connection and packet). Calls on the local player keep working; they now go through the same payload checks as a server-sent packet (finite numbers; a `rotationY` that is not a number is ignored; an avatar URL that fails the policy is ignored; `null`, `undefined` or `''` clears a session avatar).
- apps/client: an avatar URL set by a client must be an `asset://<name>.vrm` or an `http(s)://` URL of up to 512 characters, whether it is a session avatar (`setSessionAvatar`, the `avatar` connection parameter) or the equipped avatar; other values (`data:`, `blob:`, `javascript:`, ...) are ignored. `null` still clears a session avatar. The client also checks the teleport, push and session-avatar packets it receives from the server before applying them (finite numbers; a `rotationY` that is not a number is ignored).
- server: a client message over 64 KiB closes the connection (1009). `entityEvent`, `blueprintAdded`, `blueprintModified` and `ai` are capped at 64 KiB; blueprint and AI packets are builder-only, and other packets are capped at 32 KiB. Each connection has a 2048-unit bucket refilled at 1024 units/s, charging one unit plus one per full 2 KiB and one per 32 scanned nodes, including failed scans. Admission refusals are counted and logged once per kind without closing the socket.
- server: a strict plain-msgpack decoder counts nodes at admission and builds values at flush; malformed packets, unsupported extensions and trailing bytes are refused without logging content. Fair per-socket scheduling spends at most 9,216 work units per tick (`nodes + floor(bytes / 128)`), with up to about 2 s until service under a full flood at the default 64 slots. Each inbox has a 160 KiB reserve and can grow to 1 MiB from a 16 MiB shared pool.
- server: output is capped at 512 KiB per packet and 1 MiB of accounted backlog per client. The initial snapshot has its own cap of 1 MiB minus 1 KiB write overhead, with a once-per-process warning above 50%. Scripts share 512 KiB of unique output bytes per tick and skip recipients whose backlog would exceed 512 KiB; a system write that would exceed the 1 MiB cap disconnects that client. An oversized packet is refused without disconnecting recipients.
- apps: rebuild queues keep at most 64 events, with server byte/node caps per app and a shared ledger. Only a builder's next-version events may wait for an unannounced blueprint, for up to 60 seconds and within per-socket and shared budgets; delivery requires the same socket to author that version. Budget pressure drops the app's own oldest events, disconnects release reservations, and listener errors are contained.
- apps/client: the emote other players load for a player (`applyEffect({ emote })`) must be an `asset://<name>.glb` (with an optional playback query) or an `http(s)://` url of up to 512 characters; any other value shows to other players as no emote. The local effect reader now checks the same URL policy before playback and locomotion selection.
- server: a chat message's id is always minted by the server (a client-chosen id is ignored), the 500-character cut no longer splits an emoji or another grapheme, the sender's own echo goes through the same trim and cut as everyone else's copy, and a malformed chat message is logged once per connection.
- server: `/server stats` answers builders only.
- dev: lint and format are gates now. No runtime behaviour changed.
  - **`npm run check` no longer rewrites files.** It is `npm run lint && npm run format:check` (`prettier --check .`); `npm run format` is still the writer. CI runs `npm run check` where it ran `npm run lint`, so an unformatted file fails the build. The 13 tracked files that were not formatted have been formatted (whitespace only).
  - **Errors.** `no-unused-vars` (names starting with `_` are exempt, as before) and `prefer-const`, which were warnings; and, new, `no-implied-eval`, `no-new-func` and `react/no-danger` everywhere except `test/` (the test harnesses build probes with `new Function`). `no-eval` is not on yet: the number fields of the builder panes evaluate what is typed with `eval` (`src/client/components/Fields.js`), which needs a decision of its own.
  - **Warning cap.** `npm run lint` runs with `--max-warnings 51`: what is left of `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` (44; left alone, since changing a dependency array changes when an effect runs) and of `no-console` in client code (7). The number may only go down: lower it in `package.json` when a warning is fixed, never raise it. `no-console` is off where the console is the log: `src/server/`, `src/core/systems/Server*.js`, `scripts/`, `agent.mjs`, `test/` and the example app scripts.
  - **Ignore lists.** Prettier and ESLint skip vendored code (`src/core/libs/`, `src/core/physx-js-webidl.js`; Prettier also `src/core/extras/Vector3Enhanced.js`, a copy of three's Vector3 kept in three's formatting), build output, `.claude/` and the world data folders at the repository root (`/world*`), so the result is the same locally and in CI.
  - **What was cleaned up.** The 189 unused names. Unused imports and variables are removed, with the never-called `Content` component (Sidebar) and `aimBoneAt` helper (createVRMFactory); unused parameters are renamed to `_name`, never removed; a call whose result was unused is still made (the probe timer in `ServerNetwork.answersPing`, `getParameter` in `ClientGraphics`). 44 `let` became `const`.
  - **Imports kept for their side effects**, as bare `import '…'`, where the name was unused but loading the module does something: `../extras/three` (the Quaternion/Euler/BufferGeometry/Mesh/InstancedMesh prototype patches) in `UIView.js`, `NodeEnvironment.js`, `ServerNetwork.js` and `XR.js`; `../nodes/Node` in `createPlayerProxy.js` and `../extras/createVRMFactory` in `ServerLoader.js` (both reach `extras/three`); `../libs/three-custom-shader-material` in `UI.js`; `./usePane` in `AvatarPane.js` (reads the saved pane layout); `moment` in `CoreUI.js`.
  - **Checked.** The four builds before and after the cleanup: every bundle is slightly smaller, the only modules that left a bundle are `useElemSize.js` (client) and `formatBytes.js` (viewer), neither of which does anything when loaded, and the `extras/three` patches are in all six bundles as before.

### Fixed
- client, chat: a cleared chat threw in the chat line a touch device shows while the chat is closed (UI-07). A builder's `/chat clear` reaches every client (`chatCleared`), where the chat hands each of its listeners the empty list. That line (`MiniMessages`, `src/client/components/CoreUI.js`) read the sender of the list's last line and threw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'fromId')` on every touch device whose chat was closed; from the code, a desktop, or a touch device with the chat open, shows the full chat instead, which empties.
  - **What the visitor saw.** Observed in Node with the real client network (its packet queue), the real chat and the component's own source, its React hooks emulated and its timers run on a clock of the test's own (`test/miniMessages.test.js`); not in a browser. The packet queue logged the error to the console and went on: the cleared chat was empty, the line kept listening, a packet after the clear was handled, and the next line from someone else showed. A line shown 1 second before the clear stayed until its 4 seconds were over (observed on that clock), where the full chat empties at once. From the code: no error boundary took part (the throw was in the chat's listener, called from the network's tick, not in a render), and the page's own error handler did not see it (the queue caught it).
  - **Now.** An empty chat takes the line shown away, as the full chat does, and nothing is thrown. A snapshot with an empty chat (`deserialize([])`) took the same path; from the code it arrives before the interface is shown, so no visitor met it there. The other chat listener (the full chat's) and the other listeners in `CoreUI.js` read no line of a list.
  - **Tests.** `test/miniMessages.test.js`: a line, a clear, the next line and the visitor's own line through the real packet queue; the empty list handed over directly; on the test's clock, a line goes when its 4 seconds are up and not a millisecond before, a newer line starts the time again, a line after a clear keeps its own 4 seconds (the cleared line's timer is cancelled), and closing the line cancels its timer and its subscription. It fails without the fix; hand mutants are caught: the guard removed, the guard leaving the line shown, the guard without its `return`, the timer's cleanup removed, the delay changed (1, 3999 and 4001 ms), the clear leaving the old timer armed, and the subscription not given back.
  - **Checked.** The end-to-end suites fullstack and interface-faults on Chromium and WebKit, on a build of this change made as CI makes it. Neither clears the chat or runs as a touch device.
- scripts: a mesh a script made (`app.create('mesh')`, which has no material: a script cannot set one) and gave a geometry crashed its app when it was linked, the default (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'uuid')` in `Stage.insertLinked`, which keyed its batch by the material's id), with the geometry given in `app.create` or set afterwards, on the client and on the server. An app's such meshes of one geometry and the same shadow flags now share one batch, drawn with one white standard material of that app's own (the one `linkedMaterial` makes for a batch without a material of its own): a script can recolour it through its handle, so it is never shared with another app's meshes (the batch key carries a token per app entity), nor with a model's meshes of that geometry. It is let go, and disposed, with its batch, once the batch has had no mesh for 10 seconds of world time (after the app is removed, say). One that is hidden (`visible: false`) put an item without a material into the stage's octree, and a ray through it threw (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'side')`; from the code, the pointer's and the builder's raycasts go through the octree): its item has the stage's default material now.
  - **Tests.** `test/scriptMesh.test.js`, in the sweep world (the real App, Apps, Stage and Mesh node, the script in a real compartment): box, sphere and geometry types, the geometry in the options or set after the add, linked or not, client and server (24 cases); two meshes of one geometry in one batch of two with one material, apart from the model's batch, one removed leaving the other drawn; boxes of one size and spheres of one radius sharing a batch, another size or shadow flag another batch; a ray through a hidden one; two apps of one model: a batch and a material per app, app A's recolour leaving B's material and instance colours as they were, the model's own batch still shared, and A's batch and material let go (disposed once) after A is removed and the idle time has passed, B's kept. Six hand mutants are caught: the old key, a batch and a material per mesh, the material left out of the key, the hidden item without a material, the fallback shared across apps, the fallback kept after its batch is let go. The golden node files did not change. Not looked at in a browser: the fullstack and hostile-content suites pass on Chromium and WebKit with the change, but neither makes a script mesh.
- scripts, particles: five small defects of the node classes and two of the particle worker that the golden characterization found (P17-0, its "Found, not fixed"), each fixed by itself (P17-F2). Each has a test through the script's handle (the node's proxy, or `app.create` through the real Apps hook) or in the worker, in `test/nodeDefects.test.js`, which failed before its fix; 20 hand mutants that remove or weaken a fix each make its test fail. The golden files changed for the first two only (below); for the others they stayed the same, which shows only that none of the recorded cases changed.
  - **An audio node before it is in a world.** Reading `currentTime`, or calling `pause()` or `stop()`, right after `app.create('audio')` and before the node is added threw a TypeError (`this.ctx` is null; probed). They now read the world's audio through the guard `play()` already had, and act as a node in a world without audio does (the server): `currentTime` reads `0`, `pause()` and `stop()` do nothing, and a time set meanwhile is where `play()` starts once the node is added (tested with a stand-in audio context). Not changed: such a node reads `0` even after `currentTime = 5`, where a node added in a browser reads the time it was given; and its `stop()` does not put that time back to `0`, where a node added in a browser does. Its `play()` and `setPlaybackRate()` already did nothing. Of Video's getters and methods none reaches the world unguarded (`time`, `duration`, `playing`, `play()`, `pause()`, `stop()` go through its instance; the other uses are in its mount, commit and unmount). Golden: `audio.txt`, 10 lines in and 11 out, every one of `currentTime` (rows b, c, d, e, f): its reads after a set, and in row b a stopped run's context without a world, which read `undefined.audio`.
  - **A rigid body's damping.** The handle had setters and no getters for `linearDamping` and `angularDamping`: a script read `undefined` back. Each now has a getter that reads the class's, in the setter's place (the handle's key order is the same; the kind is get and set). Nothing said the write-only shape was meant: there is no comment, the class has the getters, and a prim's handle has both for its own two. `RigidBody.md` does not list either (nor `mass`), so no document said otherwise. Golden: `rigidbody.txt`, 15 lines in and 14 out (rows a, b, c, d, f, i), and two lines of `prim.txt` row b: a prim's setter called on a rigid body's handle is now recorded as setting that node, because the record reads the value back through that handle, which read `undefined` before; the setter did the same then.
  - **`UI.unmount`** cleared `needRebuild`, not `needsRebuild` (upstream 1cc9874, which meant to keep a UI from being drawn after its unmount): the node got a stray field, and a rebuild asked for before the unmount stayed asked for. A probe before the fix showed that those two were all that differed: a mount builds whatever the flag says (in a browser, not nested, not on the server), and a property set while unmounted asks again, so through a mount, a set, an unmount, a mount and a set while unmounted the build count and the canvas were the same with the typo. The test walks that path. Not changed, and not this typo's (probed after the fix, with a new UI): a UI added under another UI is refused at its mount (`ui: cannot be nested inside another ui`) and stays mounted without a canvas; a later set of one of its properties, one that asks for a rebuild or only a redraw, builds its canvas into the page anyway (a new UI has a rebuild asked for by its constructor) and then throws in the draw, which has no layout node.
  - **A node handle's pointer-callback getters** (`onPointerEnter`, `onPointerLeave`, `onPointerDown`, `onPointerUp`) checked `this.ctx` for a crashed app or a failed world. `this` was the handle, which has no `ctx`, or an object a script read the getter through (one made with `Object.create(handle)`, another receiver), whose `ctx` then decided and was read. They now check the node's. Read through the handle, nothing changes: the class getter, which is what a script got, makes the same check and more (a run that has ended), so the handle's check repeats it: four mutants that remove it pass the test.
  - **`skinnedmesh.play()`** with no options threw a TypeError (it destructured `undefined`; `stop()` has a default). It is now `play({})`, whose name is read as the text `'undefined'`: on a mounted mesh whose model has no animation of that name, what plays fades out and `[skinnedmesh] animation not found: undefined` is warned, as for any name the model has no animation of; a model with an animation named `undefined` plays it (both tested); on a mesh that is not mounted, nothing. `play(null)` still throws, as `stop(null)` does.
  - **A `box` shape of origin `volume`** drew a point and, while it was a point inside farther from every face than the thickness allows (as a share of the half side), drew again with no bound. A side of `0`, `NaN` or `Infinity`, or one whose half is `0` (`Number.MIN_VALUE`), gives a distance of `NaN` and leaves out every point inside, as does a thickness of `NaN`; sides that are all negative give distances from 1 to 2 and leave out every point at a thickness of `1` (at `1.5` and `2` points inside are taken). At a thickness of `1` or more (or `NaN`) no attempt takes the surface, and the worker recursed until "Maximum call stack size exceeded". Probed in a node worker: a side of `0`, `Number.MIN_VALUE`, `NaN` or `Infinity`, all three sides `-1` and all three `0`, each at a thickness of `1`; a side of `0` at `2`, and at `0.99999`, where an attempt takes the surface once in 100,000; a thickness of `NaN`. What that did, measured in Chromium's and WebKit's own workers (the worker bundled from the source, sent the messages the client sends): the worker lived on and answered later messages, the page got an error event (the client logs it as `[ParticleSystem]`), the other emitters kept updating, and the box emitter's update was never answered. The client sends an emitter's next update only after the answer to its last (read from the code), so that emitter kept its picture as last answered (none, when its first emit overflowed) and was not updated again. Now the sixteenth attempt takes a point of the surface, drawn as the surface branch draws one: a face at random, a point on it, the face's normal as the direction (from the centre with `spherize`, read from the code). Each particle of such a box takes all 16 attempts, 63 random draws, which the test counts. For a box with a side of `0` the two faces across that side are the whole flat box, and the four others are its edges (the test finds each particle on the face of its direction). From the code: the same box at a thickness below `1` already gave only such points (each point inside is refused, so each particle ends on the surface branch), so this is the limit of that box as the thickness rises to `1`; it is not the limit of a box whose side shrinks to `0` at a thickness of `1`, which spreads the points evenly over the flat box, each with the direction of the face nearest as a share of the half side. A side of `NaN` or `Infinity` gives `NaN` positions (probed). Below the bound the draws are the same as before, in the same order: the bound is checked first and is false there (from the code), and the test compares a box that draws again with the particles recorded before the change. How often a particle reaches the bound, from the code: for sides whose halves are normal numbers (from about 2.2e-308) and a finite thickness, the distance is spread evenly from 0 to 1, an attempt is drawn again with a chance of at most 0.11 (at a thickness of 0.25), and a particle reaches the bound with a chance below 1e-14. Not for every finite side above `0`: a side of `Number.MIN_VALUE` has a half of `0`, and every particle reaches the bound (probed); a half that is subnormal leaves the distance only a few values (at a thickness of `1` such a box takes points inside: probed with sides of 2 × `Number.MIN_VALUE` and 1e-310). The bound ends the recursion; it is not a limit on the worker's work (such a box costs 16 attempts, 63 random draws, a particle).
  - **A `timescale` of `0` with a `velocityRadial`** gave `NaN` positions from the first step: the radial step added `velocityRadial * delta`, divided by `delta`, to the velocity, which is 0 / 0 (probed with a sphere). A step of no time now adds nothing and moves nothing: the particles stay where they started. A step of any other length is computed as before, and what `timescale` accepts is unchanged. A start text such as `speed: 'Infinity'` still gives `NaN` positions (probed).
  - **Docs.** Audio (`currentTime`, `pause()` and `stop()` before the node is added), SkinnedMesh (`play()`, and a name not found), Particles (a volume box with a side of `0`).
  - **Checked.** `npm test` (3,338 tests); the end-to-end suites fullstack, hostile-content, physics-boundary and stress (200 frames) on Chromium and WebKit, on a build of this change.
- scripts: three node properties kept `NaN` or `Infinity` (P17). They now follow the rule of the bounded node properties (`src/core/extras/boundedValue.js`): `NaN` becomes the property's default, and a number past the largest float32 (about 3.4e38, `FLOAT32_MAX`), `Infinity` included, becomes that number. Nothing throws that did not throw before, and reading the property back gives what was kept.
  - **`prim.emissiveIntensity`.** It still throws, with the same message (`[prim] emissiveIntensity must be positive number`), for a negative number (`-Infinity` included) and for anything that is not a number (other than `undefined`, which gives the default, as before). It kept `NaN` and `Infinity` as given, and the value is written to the prim's slot of a Float32Array that the shader reads, which holds `Infinity` for a number from 3.4028235677973366e38 up as well: with the real Stage, that array held `NaN` and `Infinity`. With the real Stage it now holds the default (`0`) for `NaN` and the largest float32 for `Infinity`; the shader was not run. A number from just above the largest float32 up to 3.4028235677973362e38 used to be kept as given and is now kept as the largest float32, which is what the array held for it before as well. What a `NaN` or `Infinity` there did on screen was reasoned from the code and not seen on a GPU: an emissive term of `NaN` or `Infinity` in the prim's pixels, which the bloom pass (on unless a visitor turned it off) blurs into the pixels around them. A very large finite intensity, the bound included, still reaches the shader as it did; what it does on screen (a saturated prim, the bloom spreading it) was not checked on a GPU and is not bounded by this change. A material's `emissiveIntensity` already took finite numbers only. The documentation gave the default as `1`; it is `0`.
  - **`particles.direction` and `particles.velocityRadial`** are kept between about -3.4e38 and 3.4e38, as `rate`, `duration`, `max` and `timescale` are kept in their ranges; `NaN` becomes `0` for `direction` and `null` (not set) for `velocityRadial`. In the particle worker, a `direction` of `Infinity` made the directions and positions of the emitter's particles `NaN` (probed with the default cone). A `velocityRadial` of `Infinity` or `-Infinity` gave their positions a `NaN` coordinate from the first step with the default cone (`0 * Infinity` on the zero y of a particle on the cone's base), and with a sphere `Infinity` in the first step and `NaN` from the second. Each emitter computes its own particles, so another emitter's were not touched (read from the code). In the tests (a fixed sequence of random numbers, world space, the emitter at the origin, 60 steps a second): a `direction` of `Infinity` gives each particle of the default cone its drawn random offsets, normalized, as its direction (the cone's own direction is lost); a `velocityRadial` of `Infinity` moves the particles of a sphere 2 × 3.4e38 / 60 from the centre in the first step, and on the tenth step a coordinate passes the largest float32 and the position buffer holds `Infinity` there, with no `NaN` through 120 steps. The bound does not keep the worker free of `NaN` in general (see below), and it is not a protection.
  - **What a finite value does.** Within the bound it is kept as it is, a `direction` outside the documented 0 to 1 included (above 1 randomizes more, 0 or below not at all). Past the bound it now acts as the bound, and in some configurations that changes what is shown. A `direction` up to about 7.7e153 already randomized completely (in a probe with a fixed sequence of random numbers, the directions were those at the bound); above that, the length the worker divides by could overflow and particles got no direction at all (observed at 1e200); they now get a random one. A `velocityRadial` past the bound did not always take the particles out of view: in local space under a scale of 1e-38, a probe with `1e40` put the particles of a sphere about 3.33 world units from the emitter after one step, and at the bound they are about 0.113 away.
  - **Not changed.** Other numbers still reach the worker unbounded: the start values and over-life curves are texts it reads with `parseFloat` (`speed: 'Infinity'` gives `NaN` positions, `emissive: 'Infinity'` an infinite emissive), the numbers of `shape`, and the components of `force`, `velocityLinear` and `velocityOrbital`. A `timescale` of `0` with a `velocityRadial` gives `NaN` positions (the worker divides by the step's length; probed with a sphere). A `box` shape with origin `volume`, a side of `0` and a thickness of `1` or more makes the worker recurse until its stack overflows (probed); from the code, an `edge` box does not take that path. (These two are fixed since: the entry above, "(P17-F2)".) Tests: `test/nonFiniteSetters.test.js`.
- server: a stored settings row that could not be parsed made every visitor an admin, with a code set. "There is an admin code" was recorded only after the row was read successfully, and unset it counts as "no code". It is now recorded before the row is read.
- client: a file a node waits for that fails to load (missing, or refused by the loader's limits) is reported in the console instead of being left as an unhandled rejection: images, UI images, videos, avatars, a prim's texture, a particle image. Before the world was ready the page counted such a rejection as a failed start. The reason is read without running its getters (an avatar's `onLoad` can throw an object whose `message` getter throws), and anything reading it throws is caught, so the report cannot itself become an unhandled rejection.
- tooling: `npm run world:backup` archives one consistent state of the SQLite database (P13: SRV-13).
  - **The database.** tar read `db.sqlite` as it lay on disk while the server could be writing it, and with write-ahead logging the latest changes are not in that file at all: the archive could hold a database that does not open, or one without them. The database now goes in as a copy made by SQLite's own backup; the live file, its log and its lock file are left out. A database that stays locked for the whole wait fails the backup (the library reports that case as a backup of nothing, which would have been an archive without a database), and so does one written so often that the copy is not done in ten minutes. The copy has the database's permissions. With `DB_URI` (PostgreSQL) the database is not in the archive, as before, which is now said.
  - **No shell.** The tar command was a shell string built from `WORLD` and the project's path: a quote or `$(...)` in either was shell text. tar is run with its arguments as a list, and a name that begins with `-` is not read as an option.
  - **`WORLD`.** It must name a folder that exists: the backups folder was made before the check, which made the folder of a mistyped name. It is read as the server reads it, so a world beside the project (`../worlds/a`) is backed up as before; in the archive that one is now under its own name (its entries used to start with `..`).
  - **Clean-up.** Only files named as this script names them (`backup_<date>_<time>.tar.gz`) are counted and removed beyond the newest seven, by the time in the name; any `.tar.gz` with `backup_` in its name was, by the file's time. The archive just made is always one of the seven. A run that fails or is killed leaves no partial archive under a backup's name: the archive is written under a working name and gets its own at the end, by a step that does not take the name from an archive of the same second (where the disk has no hard links, a second run in that same second can still put its archive in the place of the first).
  - **Working folder.** tar reads a folder made for the run inside `<world>/backups` (`.staging-…`): the world's folder laid out again as hard links, plus the copy of the database. It needs room for that copy, uncompressed, on the world's disk; where hard links cannot be made, the files are copied and need their room too. It is removed at the end; a run that is killed leaves it, to be removed by hand. Run one backup of a world at a time.
  - **Not changed.** The archive is kept in the world's own folder, on the same disk as what it backs up: copy it elsewhere. Assets and `storage.json` are read as they are at that moment. A link inside the world's folder is archived as a link. Different in the archive: a folder its owner could not write to is one the owner can; a socket or pipe in the world's folder is left out (and named); run by a user who is neither the world's owner nor root, folders and the database have that user as their owner, which is said. The script speaks of installing tar only when tar is missing.
- server: a server that cannot start ends with exit code 1 (P13: EXIT-0, SRV-15). It used to end with 0, or stay up without listening.
  - **What happened.** SES is run with `errorTrapping: 'report'` (`src/core/lockdown.js`): it listens for uncaught exceptions, prints them and lets the process go on. For a server that is starting, a refused setting (`[envs] ...`), an unreadable database or a world that failed to load was printed and then nothing: the process ran out of work and exited with code 0, which a supervisor or a deploy reads as a clean stop, or a database connection or a timer kept it alive and it stayed up without ever listening.
  - **Now** (`src/server/startupExit.js`, the first of the server's own modules to load, so before lockdown runs: a lockdown that fails is covered too). Until the server listens the process's exit code is 1, so a start-up that ends by itself says so. Before the world loads, any uncaught exception ends the process with 1. Every step the start-up awaits (the world directory, the asset store, the database and its migrations, the clean-up, loading the world, listening on the port) ends it with 1 when it fails. While the world loads, uncaught exceptions are printed and survived, as in a running server, whatever their origin: content in a world must not be able to keep its server from starting.
  - **The exit waits for the output.** The reason is written to stderr with the error, and the process ends once stdout and stderr have handed over what was written, or after 2 s: an exit at once cut a long report short on a pipe.
  - **Not changed.** A running server still prints an uncaught exception and goes on. Start-up code that throws while the world loads outside the awaited steps (synchronously between them, or from a callback of its own) is not caught: the exit code is 1 if the process then ends by itself, and nothing ends it if something keeps it alive.
  - **Also.** `WORLD` not set is said as such (`path.join` threw its own error first). Each database migration runs in one transaction with the version it brings the database to: a step that failed halfway used to leave its first half in place and run again, on tables it had already changed, at the next start; it now leaves the database as it was (what a step does outside the database, such as storing a file, is not undone). `package.json` no longer names a `main` file that does not exist.
- client, tips: a send whose result the page did not know was reported as a failure to send. Once the wallet had been asked for the transfer, any error that was not a rejection (a WalletConnect session that dropped, a relay timeout, a wallet's internal error) and any answer that was not a transaction hash (`bad_tx`, which had no text of its own) ended on "Could not send the tip. Try again.", with the same send one press away: a payer who did as the text said could pay a second time. Such a send now ends as `outcome_unknown`: "The wallet did not give a clear answer. Your tip of 100 JPYC to Bob (0x1563…5508) may have been sent: check your wallet's activity before trying again.", with Close as the only button, so a new tip starts again from the Players list. An error before the wallet was asked to send reads "The tip was not sent. Try again."; an explicit rejection reads "Request rejected in the wallet." as before.
  - **What decides.** An explicit rejection is judged from the wallet's error and from nothing else: code 4001, which the WalletConnect adapter also gives for its wallets' rejection codes (5000 to 5003) and for an error text that says rejected, denied or cancelled. A wallet that sent the transfer and then reported a rejection is not noticed.
  - **What this is not.** The form informs the payer; it controls nothing. Anything running in the page can change both the form and the request, and a payer can still send again without looking. The form limits no amount: the built-in tokens have a maximum per tip, a custom token has one only if its spec gives it, and none of this limits what code running in the page asks a wallet for.
- tests: the token gate end-to-end suite gives its wallet panel the time the other suites give it (P14). Its wait for the panel was the suite's shortest (15 seconds on CI) and ran out now and then, after the world, the sidebar and the door had already been waited for. Why is not known: it was not reproduced, and nothing of the page was recorded at that moment. The wait is now 90 seconds on CI, as in the other suites, in two steps that name what was missing: that the world goes on ticking, then that the panel is there with a box of some size. A panel that is not there still fails; a wait whose page does not answer is reported under that wait's name.
- apps: an effect a server script takes back from a player ends on that player's client (P13: CANCEL-EFFECT). `player.cancelEffect()`, the `cancel()` of the handle `applyEffect` returns, and the clean-up of an app that is rebuilt, removed or crashes all ended the effect on the server only: nothing told the clients. The player's own client ends an effect by itself only when its `duration` is over or, for a `cancellable` one, when the player moves, so a freeze, snare, anchor or emote that a script had taken back went on there, for good when it had neither, with the server believing it over.
  - **Now.** When a server script (or its clean-up) ends an effect it gave, the clients are told (`entityModified` with `ef: null`), before the script's `onEnd` runs, so an effect that callback gives goes out after the end and not under it. Nothing is sent for a player who has left.
  - **Not sent**, as before: when the player's client itself reports the end (the server passes that on), and when another effect takes the place of the current one (the new one goes out).
  - **An effect nobody could be told of is not given.** `applyEffect` on the server admits its message before it changes anything. When the script has used up the tick's room for script writes, the player is not given the effect, keeps the one it has, and the call answers `false`; the server used to give it all the same and hold an effect no client had heard of.
  - **A script's write.** When a script asks for the end, the message counts in that tick's room for script writes, like the one that gave the effect (giving and ending in a loop is no way round it). When there is no room, nobody is told and the effect is left as it is, on the server as on the clients: `cancel()` and `cancelEffect()` answer `false` and can be called again (`false` is also the answer when the end was told to some connections and not all; the handle's `active` says whether the effect is over). The clean-up's message is the engine's own and is not refused.
  - **Whose effect.** A handle ends only the effect the player still has from it. When an effect ended, its callback used to run first and was then wiped together with anything it had set: a script whose `onEnd` gave the player another effect was left with a handle for an effect the player no longer had, and that handle's `cancel()`, or the app's clean-up, then ended whatever the player had at the time, another app's effect included. The callback now runs after the new state is in place (`PlayerRemote.setEffect`, `PlayerLocal.setEffect` and `modify`), so what it gives stays, with its own callback; and the handle checks that the player's effect is its own.
  - **A script that answers the end with another effect.** When the player's client ends an effect and the script's `onEnd` gives another, that one is the player's effect, for the server, the other clients and the player's own. The server used to drop it again and tell the others the client's end, while the player's client had been told the script's effect and kept it.
  - **A limit.** The message names no effect. A client script that gives its own player another effect at the moment a server script ends one can see the new one ended instead.
- client: a canvas arc of a negative radius no longer throws in the middle of a draw: a negative `borderRadius`, a border wider than its box, or a nametag health below zero (whose throw could leave the shared nametag atlas half drawn when another tag was removed). A box without a size draws nothing; the health bar runs from empty to full.
- engine: small defects of shared helpers (P13: XTR-05, 06, 12, 16).
  - **The scene's octree kept every node it grew.** Taking an item out of the tree (a mesh that leaves the scene) emptied its node and left it, with all the nodes above it: only an insert or a move gave empty nodes back. 20 scattered items made 513 nodes, and 513 stayed after all 20 were removed; a ray that crosses the bounds of such empty branches walks into them. What removals emptied is now given back once a frame (`collapseEmptied`, from the stage), not at the removal itself: a mesh that is rebuilt is removed and inserted again in the same frame, and collapsing in between would throw away the nodes it goes back into.
  - **A node asked to remove what it does not hold** (both octrees) took out the last item it does hold and counted it (`splice(-1, 1)`). No caller was found that does this; the node now leaves its items alone.
  - **Upload check.** The file's extension went into the query of `/api/upload-check` as it was: one with `&` or `#` in it changed the query (the size the server was told, or cut it). The name is encoded.
  - **Sizes.** `formatBytes` gave `NaN undefined` for a negative number or NaN, and a number with the unit `undefined` below one byte and above 1024 TB. What is not a size above 0 reads `0 B`; the rest is counted in bytes or in TB.
  - **Downloads.** The object URL of a downloaded file was revoked in the same turn the download was asked for, which some browsers take up later; it is revoked a minute on.
  - **Not changed, on purpose.** The snap octree does not split a node when points are inserted (they pile up in one leaf's list; only growing the tree for a point outside it makes children): said in its code now, a world has few snap points. `prng` is biased and gives negative numbers for ranges of 2^31 and more: correcting it would change every seeded sequence existing worlds were built with, so it is left for the owner to decide. A caller's abort signal keeps one listener per upload request until the batch ends (taking it off sooner would stop the abort from reaching a response still being read).
- nodes: small defects of the node classes (P13: NODE-07, 09, 10, 13, 14, 16, 20).
  - **Image.** `src` set to `null` left the previous image in the scene, and an image still loading when its `src` was cleared appeared when it arrived; the node now shows nothing. The texture made for each build was not disposed with the mesh (its copy on the GPU was left to the browser's garbage collection); it is.
  - **Audio.** Another `src` set while the audio played started the new file where the old one had got to (a track changed after a minute started a minute in). It now plays from its start, at once, and a paused node's time goes back to `0`. A `src` changed while the first file was still loading played that first file; the node plays its `src`. Setting the same `src` again, or changing `loop`, `group` or `spatial`, keeps the place, as before. **Upgrade note:** a script that swapped `src` between two files of one length to keep them in step (two mixes of a track) now hears the second from its start; read `currentTime` before the change and set it after. `pause()` now also calls off a `play()` that is still waiting for its file, as `stop()` did: `play(); pause()` before the file has arrived stays silent (it used to start when the file came), and a `play()` after it plays.
  - **Video.** A video's sound went to the `music` group whatever its `group`. It goes to the node's group: a video with `group: 'sfx'` now follows the visitor's SFX volume.
  - **Clones.** A clone lost its source's nametag `health`, rigid body `linearDamping`, `angularDamping` and centre of mass, UI image `borderRadius`, and video `onLoad`. `lod.clone()` without its children threw; it gives a LOD without levels.
  - **Skinned mesh.** After a remount (the node switched off and on) `play()` of the clip that had played before did nothing. `castShadow` and `receiveShadow` set after the mount were ignored until the next mount; they apply at once, without a rebuild, so the animation goes on. `getBone('constructor')`, or any other name every object has, returned a function, not a bone handle. The skeleton copied for each mount is disposed at the unmount.
  - **Avatar.** After `src = null` the node went on showing the avatar it had loaded; it shows none. An avatar node that came with a model has no `src` and is not affected.
  - **Controller.** A controller with `visible: true` threw when mounted, on the server and on the client: its capsule went into a scene that does not exist. The capsule goes into the stage's scene, and is not made where there are no graphics (the server).
  - **Anchor.** Two mounted anchors of one id (a clone keeps its source's id) shared one entry: the first to unmount took the anchor away from the other, and a player anchored there was no longer held. Every mounted anchor is kept (`src/core/systems/Anchors.js`: the registry is the system's, a node cannot know what else holds its id); the one mounted last answers for the id, as before.
  - **LOD.** A child removed from a LOD, or added to another node, stayed one of its levels: when the camera came within its distance again it was mounted, with no parent. `insert(node)` without a distance (or with `NaN`) put a level in the list that no distance reaches, and the list could no longer be sorted (10, NaN, 5 stayed in that order, so the level of 10 was shown at a distance of 3). Such a node is still added and never shown, as before; it is no level. A child inserted a second time has the new distance only, and one added again with `add` keeps its level.
  - **UI.** A child drawn outside its parent's box (an absolute one, or one wider than its parent; nothing is clipped) could not be hit there: the pointer went to what was drawn behind it. The hit is the node drawn last at the point, inside the canvas.
  - The error for replacing a node's `rotation` said `position`.
  - **Not changed, for the owner to decide.** A mesh without a geometry renders nothing, also with the default `type: 'box'` or with `'sphere'` (`mount` returns before it looks at the type): that is what `app.create('mesh')` gives, and making it render would put boxes into existing worlds. A video with `visible: false` never reports `loading: false`, never calls `onLoad`, and a `play()` called before its file is ready never starts (one called later does): correcting that would make such videos start to sound. A skinned mesh is still copied into the scene on the server, where scripts can read its bones.
- client: when the page's audio is unlocked by the first click, each waiting sound starts on its own; one that threw left every sound queued before it, of any app, unstarted.
- server: three leftovers of the network review (P13: PRIV-TG1, NET-15, CHAIN-11).
  - **Who builds by a token was told to every later joiner.** The mark of a session-only token grant (`tokenGrantedRank`) was kept in the player's data, and a player's data is what the join snapshot carries: everyone who joined afterwards could read which builders hold their rank by a token and which by a stored rank. The mark is on the server's player object now, not in its data. (The wallet address was never in it.)
  - **Command packets.** The server took any list for a command's words and handed it to server scripts in the `command` event: a client could put numbers and objects there. A command is now a list of strings that a chat line can hold; anything else is dropped. The event has the shape a client script gets (`{ playerId, cmd, value, args }`; the server's used to carry `args` alone): `cmd` and `value` are the client's when they are the text those words came from, and are made from the words otherwise.
  - **Token balances.** A raw balance was made a Number and then divided, which rounds twice once the raw amount is past 2^53 units (with 18 decimals, any balance above a hundredth of a token). It is now written out as a decimal and read once: the nearest number to the balance. Below 2^53 raw units the result is the same as before. A balance is still a double, so two amounts closer together than a double can tell apart read as one.
  - **Not changed.** A tip report of the wrong shape (not an object of exactly a ticket id and a transaction hash) gets no answer, as before: an honest client cannot send one.
- client, agent: small defects (P13: CSYS-07, CSYS-11, CSYS-13, CSYS-14, UI-09).
  - **A teleport at join was lost.** The server can send a teleport as soon as a player has joined (a script's spawn point), while the player's set-up still waits for its first assets. `teleport` read the pose of a body that did not exist yet and threw. The position is now taken, and the body and the camera start there.
  - **Action hints.** A check in `buildActions` that was to list one action per key was never true (`!action.type === 'custom'`) and never ran. It is removed, not turned on: every active control's actions are listed, as they always were. Which control a key goes to depends on what each one captures, so listing only the first named could show the hint of an action the key does not do.
  - **XR input sources with fewer buttons.** The trigger, the grip, the two face buttons and the thumbstick were read at fixed places of the gamepad. A source without them (a hand, a simple controller) threw on every frame, which ended the script's control. What a source lacks reads as not pressed (`src/core/extras/xrGamepad.js`).
  - **Avatar preview.** A size check with a placeholder limit of a terabyte and the label "1LOLS" is gone; nothing showed its answer. The server's limit is applied and said when the file is uploaded, as before.
  - **Agent** (`agent.mjs`, `scripts/lib/agentFiles.mjs`). `AGENT_ID` names the token file and went into its path as it was (`../../../x` wrote outside the directory): it must be 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_`, `-` or `.`, or the agent stops. The token is written to a new file readable by its owner only and renamed over the token file, so it is not in a file whose mode lets others read it, also when the token file was there before with another mode (on Windows, which has no such modes, it is written into the file that is there, as before). Other players' names and chat lines are logged without control characters (a terminal obeys escape sequences) and cut. A start that fails is said and tried again like a lost connection; it used to be a rejection nobody handled.
- wallet: a tip that was sent is still watched while other connections report junk transaction hashes (P12: CHAIN-02, N6).
  - **What happened.** The server watched 16 reported transactions in the whole world, 2 per connection, and one address may hold 8 connections: its junk hashes filled the table (each for about a minute, and a minute more on the retry), and a sender whose transfer had already left the wallet was answered "busy". With a larger table it would still have failed: a new report was asked about before every report that had been asked about once, so with 63 connections reporting junk a simulated honest tip got its first chain call and no second one, and ended `unverified` (`timeout`) at 180 s.
  - **The table.** `TIP_WATCH_MAX` is 2 for every connection the server admits: 2 x `WS_MAX_SOCKETS`, 128 by default, about 1.1 KB of memory a job (about 0.14 MB full). With `WS_MAX_SOCKETS=0` there is no number to derive it from and it stays 128.
  - **The order.** A new report waits its turn from the moment it arrives (`polledAt` starts at the arrival time, not at minus infinity). In the same simulation the honest tip is confirmed after 58 s (junk that is a mined transaction without the transfer) and 63 s (junk no node knows); alone it takes 5 s.
  - **What this is not.** A share for each sender. The chain calls stay one budget for every report: 4 calls started a second with a second call for some steps (about 4 to 5 a second in the tests; the bucket's 8 a second is the ceiling, not the rate). Enough connections reporting junk still delay every confirmation, and with a slow RPC or a late `finalized` block a tip can still end unconfirmed. A tip that is not confirmed is not announced and no script hears of it; that says nothing about the transfer itself, which may have gone through, be pending still, or never have been mined: the chain says which.
- wallet: one transfer is one tip, as far as one server process can tell (P12: CDX-11, N3). Nothing is stored and the database is unchanged.
  - **After a restart.** The transfers the server has counted are in its memory, so a restart forgot them, and a tip request made within 30 s of an already counted transfer's block (both players signed in again) matched it a second time: a second announcement and a second `tip` event, which a script may reward twice. A transfer in a block stamped earlier than the start of the running process is now refused (`too_old`); the bound is the later of the request's time less 30 s (in whole seconds, rounded down) and the first whole second after the process started.
  - **What that rests on.** Two clocks: the block times the RPC node gives must not be ahead of this machine's clock, and that clock must not have gone back. It is a rule for one server process at a time, not a record of what was counted: two processes serving the world at once (a deploy that overlaps), a wrong clock or a node that answers wrongly are outside it. In the first 30 s after a start, a tip in a block stamped earlier than the start is refused.
  - **Two senders in one transaction.** What is remembered is the transaction hash with the sender (`hash:from`, compared without regard to letter case), not the hash alone, so when one transaction carries transfers of two of this world's senders (a bundler's) the second is no longer refused as a duplicate. The same sender reporting the same hash again is still a `duplicate`, also for a second transfer of theirs in that transaction: the node's `logIndex` is not relied on. The set is bounded as before: 30 minutes an entry, cut to the newest 4096 before each new one.
  - **Not changed.** A tip whose confirmation is under way when the server restarts or is deployed may never be counted by this world, before this change and after it (the tip request went with the old process, and the page's second report of the hash is refused by the new one, `unknown_ticket`); whether the transfer itself went through is on the chain either way. The README says so, and `docs/scripting/world/World.md` says when a `tip` event can still repeat (with a new `tipId`), that some tips raise none, and that a tip does not show that two people were involved.
- client: a video node's spatial audio setters (`refDistance`, `maxDistance`, `rolloffFactor`, the cone values, `distanceModel`) reach its panner while it plays; they wrote to a field the node never had, so a change applied only at the next rebuild.
- client: a node whose position is not finite, or too large for the float a panner takes (`1e100`), no longer throws from its audio panner's ramps every frame; the panner stays where it was.
- server: a stored avatar longer than 255 characters was accepted (up to 512) although the `users.avatar` column holds 255 on PostgreSQL: there the write failed with only a line in the log while the session went on showing the avatar, and it was gone at the next join. The stored avatar is now at most 255 characters everywhere (a file of the world's store is named in far fewer; the interface only sets those). A longer reference is not taken and its sender is not told. Session avatars, which are not stored, keep 512.
- server: voice levels leaked. Every player who left got a `null` level stored and broadcast, one entry per user id ever seen, kept for the life of the process and sent in every join snapshot. A player without a level of their own has no entry now, and leaving says nothing unless a script had given them one.
- client: a screen share whose participant metadata was not JSON threw inside the voice room's event handler, and so did the end of a share that was never shown. The metadata is read as untrusted text (an object with a `screenTargetId` string of at most 128 characters); a share that names no screen is not shown, and a target that arrives or changes after the track is followed. A screen that goes away releases its track. A player's own `screenshare()` was decided by a status set only when the share was published: a stop could leave the share running, and a change of screen back to an earlier one did nothing. It is decided by what was last asked and whether a share is published, and the status follows the screen the share is on. When a player publishes two tracks of one kind, the end of the older one no longer ends the newer one. The client's voice system no longer imports the voice server's SDK (an unused import).
- client: a UI whose canvas gets no drawing context (a browser out of canvas memory) stays blank instead of throwing in every draw, also with a text in it (its layout, which measures the text on a canvas of its own, is not run). A text whose measuring canvas gets no context takes no room instead of throwing.
- client: a particle spritesheet whose frame rate is not a number, is negative or is infinite no longer stops its emitter for good (the frame was not one of the sheet, the step threw before it answered, and the emitter waited for that answer ever after).
- client: a script editor that cannot be loaded (offline, a blocker) says so and is tried again the next time it is opened; it waited for good. When Monaco's loader came and the editor's script did not, the next try resets that loader and configures it again (it does not start a second time, and it answers a module that failed with the same error until it is reset). A failure of the editor's stylesheet or default strings is still a wait for good: the loader's plugins do not pass it on.
- client: each part of the interface renders on its own (`src/client/components/ErrorBoundary.js`). A render that throws with nothing to catch it removes React's whole tree: one bad value in a chat line, a pane or the wallet button blanked every panel and the HUD for the rest of the visit, while the world kept drawing behind it. A HUD part (chat, toasts, the action prompt, touch controls, the wallet button) is tried again by itself, three times in a row at most (the count starts again once the part has stayed on screen for a minute, however late a background tab ran the tries); a sidebar pane or the avatar pane shows "This panel hit an error" with Retry; a confirm dialog that cannot be shown answers "cancel", so whoever asked is not left waiting; the interface as a whole is tried again in the same way and comes back as it was: past the loading screen, still showing a kick, a lost connection, a confirm dialog or the avatar pane that waits for an answer (`ClientUI.latest` keeps what the world told it once); when that is used up, a message with Reload shows over the world. Not caught, as before: errors in event handlers, timers and listeners on the world, which remove nothing.
- client: touch buttons that are removed while held (jump, action) are let go.
